# Daily Summary: 2025-04-16

### Can LLMs Assist Expert Elicitation for Probabilistic Causal Modeling?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10397v1)
- **Authors**: Olha Shaposhnyk, Daria Zahorska, Svetlana Yanushkevich
- **Abstract**: Objective: This study investigates the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) as an alternative to human expert elicitation for extracting structured causal knowledge and facilitating causal modeling in biometric and healthcare applications. Material and Methods: LLM-generated causal structures, specifically Bayesian networks (BNs), were benchmarked against traditional statistical methods (e.g., Bayesian Information Criterion) using healthcare datasets. Validation techniques included structural equation modeling (SEM) to verifying relationships, and measures such as entropy, predictive accuracy, and robustness to compare network structures. Results and Discussion: LLM-generated BNs demonstrated lower entropy than expert-elicited and statistically generated BNs, suggesting higher confidence and precision in predictions. However, limitations such as contextual constraints, hallucinated dependencies, and potential biases inherited from training data require further investigation. Conclusion: LLMs represent a novel frontier in expert elicitation for probabilistic causal modeling, promising to improve transparency and reduce uncertainty in the decision-making using such models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Can LLMs Assist Expert Elicitation for Probabilistic Causal Modeling?" explores the potential of Large Language Models (LLMs) as an alternative to human expert elicitation for constructing Bayesian Networks (BNs) for causal modeling, particularly in healthcare. The authors compare LLM-generated BNs against those constructed using traditional statistical methods (like BIC and PC algorithms) and human expert knowledge. The evaluation includes structural equation modeling (SEM) to validate relationships, and measures like entropy to compare network structures. The study finds that LLM-generated BNs exhibit lower entropy than those derived from expert elicitation or statistical methods, suggesting higher confidence and precision in predictions. The authors acknowledge limitations such as potential biases inherited from training data, contextual constraints, and hallucinated dependencies.  The paper concludes that LLMs represent a promising tool for improving transparency and reducing uncertainty in causal modeling-based decision making. A case study is presented based on the 'Sleep, Health and Lifestyle' dataset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a moderately novel application of LLMs.  While the use of LLMs in causal discovery and statistical analysis has been explored, using them specifically as a *tool for expert elicitation*, effectively acting as a proxy expert and generating causal graph structures *for* subsequent BN construction, presents a useful incremental advance. The approach of using a dual-LLM system (one to propose causal links, another to verify them) is a pragmatic way to mitigate potential hallucinations and inconsistencies. However, this is not a fundamentally groundbreaking approach.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to address the limitations of traditional expert elicitation and purely data-driven causal discovery. Expert elicitation can be time-consuming, subjective, and constrained by the availability of expertise. Data-driven methods often lack the domain knowledge crucial for accurate causal inference. The LLM approach offers a potential middle ground by leveraging the broad knowledge base of LLMs while mitigating the risks of purely statistical approaches. This could lead to more efficient and transparent causal model development, potentially enhancing decision support systems in healthcare. However, the practical impact hinges on the LLM's ability to avoid biases and hallucinations and on the scalability of the approach to more complex real-world problems.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper clearly defines the problem and provides a well-structured methodology.
    *   The dual-LLM verification approach is a practical strength that addresses potential LLM limitations.
    *   The use of established validation techniques (SEM, entropy comparisons) strengthens the evaluation.
    *   The 'Sleep, Health and Lifestyle' dataset case study provides a concrete demonstration of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The case study is based on a relatively small and simple dataset. The generalizability of the findings to more complex, high-dimensional datasets needs further investigation.
    *   The paper acknowledges limitations related to biases and hallucinations, but the mitigation strategies are not fully explored. The reliance on prompt engineering, while helpful, might not be sufficient for ensuring robust and reliable results.
    *   The criteria for selecting and prompting the LLMs are not extensively detailed. It is difficult to determine the sensitivity of the results to different prompt designs.
    *   The evaluation could benefit from a comparison to a true human expert elicitation baseline. Without this, it's hard to quantify the improvement (if any) provided by the LLM-assisted approach.
    *   The reliance on pre-trained LLMs might limit the approach to domains where the LLM has sufficient prior knowledge.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could stimulate further research on using LLMs for knowledge elicitation and causal model construction. It provides a useful starting point for developing more sophisticated LLM-assisted tools for decision support. The dual-LLM verification strategy could be adopted in other applications where LLM reliability is a concern.

**Rationale for the score:**
The paper introduces a useful incremental advancement with potential but it also has several weaknesses. While the idea of using LLMs to streamline expert elicitation is valuable, the limited case study, the not-fully-addressed limitations of biases and hallucinations and lack of comparison to a real expert, mean that the paper is more of a proof of concept than a practical and robust methodology. The novelty is modest, and the significance is promising but dependent on future research addressing the noted weaknesses.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Performance of Large Language Models in Supporting Medical Diagnosis and Treatment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10405v1)
- **Authors**: Diogo Sousa, Guilherme Barbosa, Catarina Rocha, Dulce Oliveira
- **Abstract**: The integration of Large Language Models (LLMs) into healthcare holds significant potential to enhance diagnostic accuracy and support medical treatment planning. These AI-driven systems can analyze vast datasets, assisting clinicians in identifying diseases, recommending treatments, and predicting patient outcomes. This study evaluates the performance of a range of contemporary LLMs, including both open-source and closed-source models, on the 2024 Portuguese National Exam for medical specialty access (PNA), a standardized medical knowledge assessment. Our results highlight considerable variation in accuracy and cost-effectiveness, with several models demonstrating performance exceeding human benchmarks for medical students on this specific task. We identify leading models based on a combined score of accuracy and cost, discuss the implications of reasoning methodologies like Chain-of-Thought, and underscore the potential for LLMs to function as valuable complementary tools aiding medical professionals in complex clinical decision-making.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper evaluates the performance of 21 large language models (LLMs) on the 2024 Portuguese National Exam for medical specialty access (PNA), a standardized medical knowledge assessment. The study assesses both open-source and closed-source models, focusing on accuracy and cost-effectiveness.  It investigates the impact of factors like knowledge cutoff dates and reasoning methodologies like Chain-of-Thought (CoT).  The authors propose a composite scoring metric that balances accuracy, cost, and potential data contamination risk. The results compare the performance of LLMs against human student benchmarks and identify models that offer a good balance between performance and cost. The paper also discusses challenges and ethical considerations for deploying LLMs in healthcare.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study provides a relatively comprehensive evaluation of a wide range of contemporary LLMs on a standardized medical exam, including several recently released models.
    *   **Cost-Effectiveness Analysis:** The explicit consideration of cost-effectiveness is a valuable contribution, as it's a critical factor for real-world deployment in resource-constrained settings like healthcare.
    *   **Contextualization:** The discussion of knowledge cutoff dates and potential data contamination adds crucial context to the performance results. It acknowledges the possibility that some models might be "memorizing" the exam content rather than genuinely reasoning.
    *   **Practical Focus:**  The discussion of reasoning methodologies (CoT, CoD) and their implications for transparency, latency, and token costs has real-world significance.
    *   **Balanced Perspective:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of LLMs and emphasizes their role as assistive tools rather than replacements for human clinicians.
    *   **EU AI Act:** The inclusion of the EU AI Act discussion and mapping of LLMs to different risk categories adds significant value considering the increasing regulations on AI deployment, especially in high-risk sectors like healthcare.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The evaluation uses a single, multiple-choice exam in Portuguese.  This limits the generalizability of the findings to other medical domains, question formats, and languages. It's also focused on *declarative* knowledge recall, not higher-level clinical reasoning or application.
    *   **Simplistic Prompting:**  The study deliberately avoids sophisticated prompt engineering, which, while providing a baseline, potentially underestimates the true capabilities of some models.  It does not explore how few-shot learning, CoT instructions, or other methods could boost performance.
    *   **Pass@1 Methodology:**  The strict pass@1 methodology is conservative but doesn't reflect scenarios where multiple attempts or interactive dialogue are possible. It prioritizes recall over precision, which does not truly mirror the demands of diagnosis where precision is crucial.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis Missing:** The lack of a systematic qualitative error analysis is a significant weakness. Understanding the types of mistakes LLMs make (e.g., reasoning errors, knowledge gaps, hallucinations) is crucial for assessing their reliability and safety. There's only an anecdote to address this point.
    *   **Limited Novelty in Framework**: The formula incorporates some parameters that are somewhat arbitrary in their selected values and power, and a stronger validation of this scoring is needed.
    *   **Ethical and Contamination Assessment Limited**: the contamination assessment is limited to theoretical overlap, but some additional analysis on how common PNA data is, and how this changes the scores would increase the rigor of the claims being made, and increase the trustworthiness of models with low risk scores.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Calibration**: Because the scoring function prioritizes accuracy over precision, this also does not make the claim that models are making calibrated claims - and with hallucinations often appearing plausibly correct, this remains a problem.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**
    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of literature evaluating LLMs in the medical domain. The comprehensive model evaluation and cost-effectiveness analysis are valuable contributions, especially the explicit mapping with regards to the EU AI Act framework. However, the limitations discussed above restrict the generalizability and significance of the findings. Similar studies have been done.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **6.5** to this paper.

The paper provides a relatively thorough, cost-aware evaluation of current LLMs on a Portuguese medical exam, contributing a snapshot of model capabilities in that specific setting. The EU AI Act framework is interesting. However, the limited generalizability of the findings and lack of qualitative error analysis limit the overall significance. The use of simplistic prompting further caps the potential impact, as it does not explore how strategic prompting might significantly alter the landscape of performance. The novelty is moderate. The findings are relevant to the specific exam and language, but the limitations restrict its wide scale value.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL

### LLM-SRBench: A New Benchmark for Scientific Equation Discovery with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10415v1)
- **Authors**: Parshin Shojaee, Ngoc-Hieu Nguyen, Kazem Meidani, Amir Barati Farimani, Khoa D Doan, Chandan K Reddy
- **Abstract**: Scientific equation discovery is a fundamental task in the history of scientific progress, enabling the derivation of laws governing natural phenomena. Recently, Large Language Models (LLMs) have gained interest for this task due to their potential to leverage embedded scientific knowledge for hypothesis generation. However, evaluating the true discovery capabilities of these methods remains challenging, as existing benchmarks often rely on common equations that are susceptible to memorization by LLMs, leading to inflated performance metrics that do not reflect discovery. In this paper, we introduce LLM-SRBench, a comprehensive benchmark with 239 challenging problems across four scientific domains specifically designed to evaluate LLM-based scientific equation discovery methods while preventing trivial memorization. Our benchmark comprises two main categories: LSR-Transform, which transforms common physical models into less common mathematical representations to test reasoning beyond memorized forms, and LSR-Synth, which introduces synthetic, discovery-driven problems requiring data-driven reasoning. Through extensive evaluation of several state-of-the-art methods, using both open and closed LLMs, we find that the best-performing system so far achieves only 31.5% symbolic accuracy. These findings highlight the challenges of scientific equation discovery, positioning LLM-SRBench as a valuable resource for future research.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LLM-SRBench, a new benchmark for evaluating the scientific equation discovery capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). The benchmark addresses limitations of existing benchmarks, which are often susceptible to memorization by LLMs, leading to inflated performance metrics that don't accurately reflect true discovery.  LLM-SRBench consists of 239 challenging problems across four scientific domains (chemistry, biology, physics, material science). It has two main categories: LSR-Transform (transforms common physical models into less common mathematical representations) and LSR-Synth (introduces synthetic, discovery-driven problems). The paper evaluates state-of-the-art LLM-based methods using both open and closed LLMs, finding that the best-performing system achieves only 31.5% symbolic accuracy. This highlights the challenges of scientific equation discovery and positions LLM-SRBench as a valuable resource for future research. The authors contribute a novel benchmark design preventing LLM memorization with high symbolic accuracy performance evaluation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the creation of the LLM-SRBench benchmark itself. Existing benchmarks, particularly those based on the Feynman equations, are increasingly susceptible to memorization by LLMs. LSR-Transform creatively addresses this by reformulating existing physics problems in less common mathematical forms. LSR-Synth takes a more aggressive approach by incorporating synthetic terms. The combination offers a more robust evaluation of LLM's reasoning capabilities. The authors do an excellent job highlighting the limitations of prior works, and build upon previous ad-hoc attempts to mitigate memorization through a systematic benchmark creation process.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from addressing a critical gap in the evaluation of LLMs for scientific tasks. The current reliance on memorization-prone benchmarks hinders the accurate assessment of LLMs' true discovery capabilities. LLM-SRBench aims to provide a more reliable and challenging testbed. The creation of this benchmark could lead to the development of LLMs that are not just good at recalling existing equations but are capable of genuine data-driven discovery. The benchmark spanning four scientific domains makes it more comprehensive than existing resources. The inclusion of out-of-domain testing provides a very useful metric for evaluating generalization potential.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined benchmark creation methodologies (LSR-Transform and LSR-Synth).
    *   The systematic evaluation of multiple LLM models (open and closed) on the benchmark.
    *   The inclusion of both numeric and symbolic accuracy metrics, offering a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   The provision of a publicly available benchmark (github) which promote reproducibility and facilitate future research.
    *   The validation study that compared GPT-4's evaluation of symbolic equations to the evaluations of human subject matter experts.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the transformation of Feynman equations is a good starting point, the LSR-Transform still relies on existing knowledge, which could still be partially encoded in LLMs. The synthetic terms in LSR-Synth help, but a deeper analysis of their plausibility might be beneficial.
    *   The size of the benchmark (239 problems) while substantial, could benefit from future expansion.
    *   The evaluation relies on GPT-4 for assessing symbolic accuracy. Although validated, this introduces some subjectivity. Future work might explore alternative or ensemble-based evaluators.
    *   The best performing LLM is not that great, as its symbolic accuracy performance still hovers under 35%. It is not entirely clear whether the limitations are due to the data, models or the evaluation protocols.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   LLM-SRBench could become a standard benchmark for evaluating LLMs in scientific equation discovery.
    *   It could drive the development of new LLM architectures and training strategies that are less prone to memorization and more capable of true scientific reasoning.
    *   The benchmark methodology itself (particularly LSR-Transform and LSR-Synth) could inspire the creation of similar benchmarks in other scientific domains.

**Justification:**
While LLM-SRBench has some limitations, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, and the significance in addressing a crucial challenge in the field of AI and science is considerable. The systematic design, the comprehensive evaluation, the creation of non-trivial synthetic datasets, and public availability of the resources are major assets. The low performance of tested LLMs highlights that while powerful, the current LLM scientific discovery is limited by memorization, data-driven inference and scientific reasoning capabilities. Therefore, LLM-SRBench is a valuable resource for future research.

### CliniChat: A Multi-Source Knowledge-Driven Framework for Clinical Interview Dialogue Reconstruction and Evaluation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10418v1)
- **Authors**: Jing Chen, Zhihua Wei, Wei Zhang, Yingying Hu, Qiong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) hold great promise for assisting clinical interviews due to their fluent interactive capabilities and extensive medical knowledge. However, the lack of high-quality interview dialogue data and widely accepted evaluation methods has significantly impeded this process. So we propose CliniChat, a framework that integrates multi-source knowledge to enable LLMs to simulate real-world clinical interviews. It consists of two modules: Clini-Recon and Clini-Eval, each responsible for reconstructing and evaluating interview dialogues, respectively. By incorporating three sources of knowledge, Clini-Recon transforms clinical notes into systematic, professional, and empathetic interview dialogues. Clini-Eval combines a comprehensive evaluation metric system with a two-phase automatic evaluation approach, enabling LLMs to assess interview performance like experts. We contribute MedQA-Dialog, a high-quality synthetic interview dialogue dataset, and CliniChatGLM, a model specialized for clinical interviews. Experimental results demonstrate that CliniChatGLM's interview capabilities undergo a comprehensive upgrade, particularly in history-taking, achieving state-of-the-art performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CliniChat: A Multi-Source Knowledge-Driven Framework for Clinical Interview Dialogue Reconstruction and Evaluation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CliniChat, a framework designed to improve the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to simulate realistic clinical interviews. The framework tackles the challenges of limited high-quality interview dialogue data and the lack of standardized evaluation methods. CliniChat consists of two main modules:

*   **Clini-Recon:**  This module reconstructs clinical interview dialogues from clinical notes using a multi-source knowledge approach. It incorporates knowledge from patient interview guidelines, LLMs, and physicians to generate systematic, professional, and empathetic dialogues.
*   **Clini-Eval:** This module focuses on evaluating the generated interview dialogues using a comprehensive metric system and a two-phase evaluation approach called Demo2Eval. Demo2Eval leverages LLMs to assess interview performance, mimicking expert evaluations.

The authors contribute MedQA-Dialog, a synthetic dataset of high-quality interview dialogues created using Clini-Recon, and CliniChatGLM, an LLM fine-tuned for clinical interviews using this dataset. Experimental results demonstrate that CliniChat-GLM significantly improves interview capabilities, especially in history-taking, achieving state-of-the-art performance compared to existing LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The paper offers several novel contributions:

*   **Comprehensive Framework:** The integration of both a dialogue reconstruction and evaluation module (Clini-Recon and Clini-Eval) provides a more holistic approach than many existing works which focus only on dialogue generation.
*   **Multi-Source Knowledge Integration:** The use of patient interview guidelines, LLMs, and physician input in Clini-Recon is a strong point, leading to more realistic and professional dialogues than approaches relying solely on LLMs.
*   **Demo2Eval Evaluation Method:**  The Demo2Eval approach, mimicking clinical interview training, is a novel way to automatically assess LLM performance in this domain.
*   **MedQA-Dialog Dataset:** The generation of a synthetic dialogue dataset fills a crucial gap, addressing the scarcity of high-quality clinical interview data.
*   **CliniChat-GLM:** Showing the benefits of fine-tuning existing LLMs, on the constructed data and evaluation framework, showcases potential of LLMs in clinical settings.

*Strengths:*

*   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of current LLMs in simulating clinical interviews and the challenges in developing effective assisted clinical interview systems.
*   **Detailed Methodology:** The paper provides detailed explanations of Clini-Recon and Clini-Eval, including the knowledge sources used, planning strategies, and evaluation metrics.
*   **Extensive Experiments:** The experiments include both intrinsic (dialogue quality) and extrinsic (LLM interview performance) evaluations, providing robust evidence of CliniChat's effectiveness. The comparisons against existing LLMs and role-playing methods further strengthen the findings.
*   **Performance Gains:** The reported performance gains, particularly in history taking, are significant and demonstrate the potential of CliniChat to improve LLM capabilities in this area.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Reliance on Synthetic Data:**  While MedQA-Dialog addresses data scarcity, the inherent limitations of synthetic data remain. The dialogues may not fully capture the complexity and nuances of real-world clinical interactions.
*   **Evaluation Limitations:** The evaluation primarily relies on the Clini-Eval framework guided by GPT-4, meaning that the results will only be as good as how those LLMs perform. Expert evaluation is stated in the limitations but is not included as part of this paper.
*   **Generalizability:** The reliance on GLM-4-Air and other Chinese-specific models may limit the generalizability of the findings to other languages and cultural contexts.
*   **Cost and Accessibility:** While CliniChat reduces reliance on very large LLMs, cost and accessibility are still factors, particularly for researchers without access to the specific models used.

*Potential Influence on the Field:*

CliniChat has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM-assisted clinical interviews by providing:

*   A framework for generating more realistic and high-quality interview dialogues.
*   A comprehensive evaluation methodology for assessing LLM performance.
*   A valuable synthetic dataset for training and evaluating LLMs.
*   A stepping stone towards developing more effective and reliable assisted clinical interview systems.

Given the need for improved data and evaluation methods in this domain, CliniChat's contributions are timely and relevant.

**Overall Score:**

Score: 7.5

*Rationale:*

The paper presents a well-designed framework with several novel components and demonstrates significant performance improvements compared to existing methods. The multi-source knowledge integration in Clini-Recon and the Demo2Eval evaluation method are particularly strong contributions. The generation of the MedQA-Dialog dataset is also a valuable resource.

However, the limitations of relying solely on synthetic data and GPT-4-guided evaluations, as well as the potential for limited generalizability, prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. The absence of expert evaluation impacts the confidence in the results. Future work should focus on validating the framework with real-world data, incorporating diverse LLMs and evaluation methods, and conducting user studies to assess the clinical utility of CliniChat.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Unchecked and Overlooked: Addressing the Checkbox Blind Spot in Large Language Models with CheckboxQA
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10419v2)
- **Authors**: Michał Turski, Mateusz Chiliński, Łukasz Borchmann
- **Abstract**: Checkboxes are critical in real-world document processing where the presence or absence of ticks directly informs data extraction and decision-making processes. Yet, despite the strong performance of Large Vision and Language Models across a wide range of tasks, they struggle with interpreting checkable content. This challenge becomes particularly pressing in industries where a single overlooked checkbox may lead to costly regulatory or contractual oversights. To address this gap, we introduce the CheckboxQA dataset, a targeted resource designed to evaluate and improve model performance on checkbox-related tasks. It reveals the limitations of current models and serves as a valuable tool for advancing document comprehension systems, with significant implications for applications in sectors such as legal tech and finance. The dataset is publicly available at: https://github.com/Snowflake-Labs/CheckboxQA
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Unchecked and Overlooked: Addressing the Checkbox Blind Spot in Large Language Models with CheckboxQA" identifies a significant deficiency in the performance of large vision and language models (LVLMs) when it comes to interpreting checkboxes in visually rich documents. The authors introduce CheckboxQA, a new dataset specifically designed to evaluate and improve models' ability to understand and reason about checkbox content. The paper details the dataset creation process, including document selection, annotation, and validation.  It also presents baseline results using various commercial and open-source LVLMs, demonstrating their subpar performance compared to human annotators.  The paper concludes with a qualitative analysis of common failure modes and a discussion of limitations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on checkbox interpretation within the context of Document VQA. While general Document VQA datasets exist, this work highlights a previously under-addressed aspect of document understanding. Isolating this problem is valuable. The authors' approach of building a dedicated dataset addresses a clear gap in existing benchmarks.
* **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from the practical importance of accurate checkbox interpretation in real-world applications. The authors correctly point out that errors in checkbox understanding can have significant consequences in areas like legal and financial document processing. This justifies the focus and makes the research immediately applicable.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Well-defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of checkbox interpretation and its importance.
    * **Targeted Dataset:** The CheckboxQA dataset is a valuable contribution, as it provides a dedicated resource for evaluating and improving models on this specific task. The detailed explanation of the data collection and annotation process strengthens the reproducibility of the work.
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough evaluation of various LVLMs, providing a benchmark for future research.
    * **Qualitative Analysis:** The qualitative analysis of failure modes offers valuable insights into the challenges that models face and can guide future research directions.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Dataset Size:** While useful, the dataset size (88 documents, 579 QA pairs) could be a limitation. Scaling up the dataset would make the benchmark even more robust.
    * **English Only:** Restricting the data to English limits its wider applicability across various languages.
    * **Lack of Novel Model Contribution:** The paper focuses on dataset creation and evaluation. While useful, it does not propose a novel model architecture or training strategy specifically designed to address the checkbox interpretation problem.

* **Potential Influence:** The CheckboxQA dataset is likely to be adopted by the Document AI research community as a benchmark for evaluating checkbox interpretation capabilities. This could drive further research into developing more robust and accurate models for document understanding.

Overall, the paper makes a solid contribution by highlighting a previously neglected but important aspect of Document AI and providing a valuable resource for future research. While the dataset could be larger and more diverse, the paper's focus and clarity are commendable. The absence of a novel modeling contribution slightly reduces its impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL

### Can We Edit LLMs for Long-Tail Biomedical Knowledge?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10421v1)
- **Authors**: Xinhao Yi, Jake Lever, Kevin Bryson, Zaiqiao Meng
- **Abstract**: Knowledge editing has emerged as an effective approach for updating large language models (LLMs) by modifying their internal knowledge. However, their application to the biomedical domain faces unique challenges due to the long-tailed distribution of biomedical knowledge, where rare and infrequent information is prevalent. In this paper, we conduct the first comprehensive study to investigate the effectiveness of knowledge editing methods for editing long-tail biomedical knowledge. Our results indicate that, while existing editing methods can enhance LLMs' performance on long-tail biomedical knowledge, their performance on long-tail knowledge remains inferior to that on high-frequency popular knowledge, even after editing. Our further analysis reveals that long-tail biomedical knowledge contains a significant amount of one-to-many knowledge, where one subject and relation link to multiple objects. This high prevalence of one-to-many knowledge limits the effectiveness of knowledge editing in improving LLMs' understanding of long-tail biomedical knowledge, highlighting the need for tailored strategies to bridge this performance gap.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the effectiveness of knowledge editing techniques for improving Large Language Models' (LLMs) performance on long-tail biomedical knowledge.  It finds that while knowledge editing can improve LLMs' ability to recall rare biomedical facts, the performance remains lower compared to more frequent, popular knowledge. A key finding is that long-tail biomedical knowledge often involves one-to-many relationships between entities, which poses a significant challenge for current knowledge editing methods. The study suggests that tailored strategies are needed to address the complexities of long-tail biomedical knowledge and improve LLMs' understanding in this domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel contribution by specifically focusing on the challenges of editing *long-tail* knowledge in the *biomedical* domain.  Previous research on knowledge editing has primarily concentrated on general-purpose knowledge or did not explicitly address the long-tail distribution.  Examining the impact of one-to-many relationships on editing performance is also a valuable insight. This is a significant contribution since medicine exhibits a long-tail of information.

*   **Significance:** The work has practical significance. LLMs are increasingly used in healthcare for diagnosis and treatment recommendations. Improving their ability to handle rare but critical biomedical information is vital for reliable clinical applications. Identifying the challenges posed by one-to-many relationships offers a direction for future research aimed at bridging the performance gap between common and rare knowledge.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **First comprehensive study:** Claims to be the first to comprehensively examine knowledge editing for long-tail biomedical knowledge, which lends to its importance.
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of long-tail knowledge and its implications for LLMs in biomedicine.
    *   **Well-defined experiments:** The authors design experiments to evaluate the effectiveness of various knowledge editing methods. They use standard datasets (SNOMED CT, PubMed) and metrics (Accuracy, Reliability, Generalization, Locality) that adds confidence in their findings.
    *   **Insightful analysis:** The analysis of one-to-many relationships as a factor limiting performance is a valuable contribution.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Providing access to the constructed dataset and their pipeline is a strong positive for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited solutions:** The paper primarily focuses on diagnosing the problem rather than proposing and evaluating novel solutions. While identifying challenges is crucial, the lack of experimental validation of new methods limits the immediate practical impact. It leaves readers wondering where to go from here.
    *   **Simplistic Evaluation**: A common problem when evaluating knowledge editing methods is that it is hard to confirm true edits. Their accuracy metrics don't guarantee that models aren't simply memorizing phrases instead of understanding the underlying relationship being presented.
    *   **Reliance on co-occurrence**: Defining long-tail knowledge based solely on document co-occurrence is a rather crude measure, and might conflate true rarity with noise. There is a risk that relevant biomedical concepts aren't well-documented, which causes them to be identified as "long-tail" when it really is just an documentation artifact.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper's findings can guide future research in knowledge editing. It highlights the need for methods that can effectively handle one-to-many relationships and long-tail distributions. It could lead to the development of more robust and reliable LLMs for biomedical applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's novelty lies in its specific focus on long-tail biomedical knowledge and the insight it provides into the challenges posed by one-to-many relationships. Its significance is tied to the growing use of LLMs in healthcare. The study is well-designed, and the analysis is insightful. However, the limited exploration of novel solutions and relatively simplistic evaluation somewhat diminishes its overall impact. While the issue is identified in a solid manner, the "so what?" is lacking.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### LLM Can be a Dangerous Persuader: Empirical Study of Persuasion Safety in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10430v1)
- **Authors**: Minqian Liu, Zhiyang Xu, Xinyi Zhang, Heajun An, Sarvech Qadir, Qi Zhang, Pamela J. Wisniewski, Jin-Hee Cho, Sang Won Lee, Ruoxi Jia, Lifu Huang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have enabled them to approach human-level persuasion capabilities. However, such potential also raises concerns about the safety risks of LLM-driven persuasion, particularly their potential for unethical influence through manipulation, deception, exploitation of vulnerabilities, and many other harmful tactics. In this work, we present a systematic investigation of LLM persuasion safety through two critical aspects: (1) whether LLMs appropriately reject unethical persuasion tasks and avoid unethical strategies during execution, including cases where the initial persuasion goal appears ethically neutral, and (2) how influencing factors like personality traits and external pressures affect their behavior. To this end, we introduce PersuSafety, the first comprehensive framework for the assessment of persuasion safety which consists of three stages, i.e., persuasion scene creation, persuasive conversation simulation, and persuasion safety assessment. PersuSafety covers 6 diverse unethical persuasion topics and 15 common unethical strategies. Through extensive experiments across 8 widely used LLMs, we observe significant safety concerns in most LLMs, including failing to identify harmful persuasion tasks and leveraging various unethical persuasion strategies. Our study calls for more attention to improve safety alignment in progressive and goal-driven conversations such as persuasion.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LLM Can be a Dangerous Persuader: Empirical Study of Persuasion Safety in Large Language Models."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PERSUSAFETY, a framework for evaluating the safety of Large Language Models (LLMs) in persuasion scenarios. It investigates whether LLMs can refuse unethical requests and avoid unethical strategies, even when the initial goal seems neutral. The framework includes three stages: task generation (creating both ethical and unethical persuasion scenarios), conversation simulation (simulating multi-turn interactions between LLM persuaders and persuadees), and safety assessment (measuring refusal rates and unethical strategy usage). The authors conduct experiments on 8 widely used LLMs, finding that most models fail to consistently reject harmful tasks and often employ unethical persuasion tactics. They also show that LLMs strategically adapt and intensify unethical techniques when aware of persuadee vulnerabilities and that external pressures can compromise ethical boundaries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a critical but largely unexplored area: the safety of LLMs as persuaders, especially in goal-driven conversations. This is a timely and important topic as LLMs become increasingly capable.
    *   **Framework:** PERSUSAFETY is a comprehensive and well-structured framework for evaluating persuasion safety. The inclusion of different factors that could affect it (personalities, context, etc.) is relevant.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The study performs extensive experiments across multiple LLMs, providing insights into their behavior under various conditions, like the visibility of vulnerabilities, and in both unethical and ethical scenarios.
    *   **Real-world relevance:** The study considers real-world aspects of safety in persuasion through experiments with persuading for unethical goals and ethically neutral goals when the persuadee's vulnerabilities are exposed.
    *   **Important Findings:** The findings are concerning and highlight the potential for LLMs to be exploited for harmful purposes. The gap between task refusal and ethical execution (e.g., Claude being good at refusing, but still uses unethical strategies if it accepts the request) is particularly alarming.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The research clearly frames the problem and makes well-defined contributions that are important for developing LLM-based agents.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Simulation Limitations:** The study relies on simulated conversations, which may not fully capture the complexities of human interactions. While this is necessary for controlled experiments, it limits the generalizability of the findings to real-world scenarios. The prompts used for each role could still be improved to reflect more realistic behavior.
    *   **Strategy Assessment:** The assessment of unethical strategies is largely automated, relying on Claude-3.5-Sonnet as a judge. Although the automated assessment is checked by human evaluators, relying on one system limits the ability to find mistakes.
    *   **Scope of Strategies:** While the taxonomy of unethical strategies is useful, it is not exhaustive. Future work could expand this taxonomy to include a wider range of manipulative tactics.
    *   **Limited Mitigation Strategies:** The paper focuses on identifying safety risks but offers limited concrete solutions for mitigating them. While the authors acknowledge the need for further research in this area, the absence of preliminary mitigation strategies is a drawback.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Impact on the Field:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of NLP and AI safety. It raises important ethical considerations for the development and deployment of persuasive LLMs.
    *   **Future Research Directions:** The study opens up several avenues for future research, including the development of more robust safety mechanisms, the creation of more comprehensive evaluation frameworks, and the exploration of human-AI collaboration in persuasive contexts.

* **Overall score and justification:**

Overall, the paper makes a valuable contribution by systematically analyzing the persuasion safety of LLMs. The novel framework and insightful findings highlight significant ethical concerns and suggest directions for future research. However, the study is limited by its reliance on simulations and the lack of concrete mitigation strategies.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rigorous Rationale:** The score reflects the paper's strong novelty and comprehensive framework (earning it points for significance), but acknowledges the limitations of its simulation-based approach and lack of concrete mitigation strategies (slightly detracting from its overall impact). The findings on exploiting vulnerabilities are alarming and support the need for further work to address these issues.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### MonoDiff9D: Monocular Category-Level 9D Object Pose Estimation via Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10433v1)
- **Authors**: Jian Liu, Wei Sun, Hui Yang, Jin Zheng, Zichen Geng, Hossein Rahmani, Ajmal Mian
- **Abstract**: Object pose estimation is a core means for robots to understand and interact with their environment. For this task, monocular category-level methods are attractive as they require only a single RGB camera. However, current methods rely on shape priors or CAD models of the intra-class known objects. We propose a diffusion-based monocular category-level 9D object pose generation method, MonoDiff9D. Our motivation is to leverage the probabilistic nature of diffusion models to alleviate the need for shape priors, CAD models, or depth sensors for intra-class unknown object pose estimation. We first estimate coarse depth via DINOv2 from the monocular image in a zero-shot manner and convert it into a point cloud. We then fuse the global features of the point cloud with the input image and use the fused features along with the encoded time step to condition MonoDiff9D. Finally, we design a transformer-based denoiser to recover the object pose from Gaussian noise. Extensive experiments on two popular benchmark datasets show that MonoDiff9D achieves state-of-the-art monocular category-level 9D object pose estimation accuracy without the need for shape priors or CAD models at any stage. Our code will be made public at https://github.com/CNJianLiu/MonoDiff9D.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MonoDiff9D: Monocular Category-Level 9D Object Pose Estimation via Diffusion Model" proposes a novel method for estimating the 9D pose (translation, rotation, and size) of objects from a single RGB image, without relying on shape priors or CAD models. The approach uses a pre-trained large vision model (DINOv2) to estimate coarse depth from the input image and converts it to a point cloud. This point cloud and the RGB image are then used as conditions to guide a diffusion model. A transformer-based denoiser recovers the object pose from Gaussian noise.  The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on standard datasets (CAMERA25 and REAL275) without using shape priors or CAD models. The code is made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the combination of several existing components into a single pipeline for monocular 9D pose estimation *without* relying on CAD models or shape priors. The use of DINOv2 for coarse depth estimation in a zero-shot manner to condition a diffusion model is an interesting application. Diffusion models have been used for pose estimation before, but typically not in this way, nor without shape priors.
* **Significance:** Category-level pose estimation is a valuable task for robotics and augmented reality. Monocular methods are desirable due to the ubiquity of RGB cameras. The *potential* significance of this work is in alleviating the dependency on shape priors or CAD models.  Acquiring these priors can be a significant bottleneck. A method that performs well without them has practical value. This is particularly relevant in environments where CAD models are either unavailable or inaccurate.  However, the extent of this significance is somewhat limited by the fact that DINOv2, while not a *CAD* model or *shape prior* in the traditional sense, has been extensively pre-trained on a massive dataset and effectively encodes a broad prior about the structure of the world.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Shape Prior-Free:** A key strength is the ability to perform pose estimation without explicit shape priors or CAD models. This is a step toward greater generalizability.
    * **Competitive Performance:** The method achieves state-of-the-art results on standard benchmarks, showing its effectiveness.
    * **Well-Documented Experiments:** The paper includes ablation studies to analyze the contribution of different components, providing insights into the method's workings.
    * **Reproducibility:** The code being made public is a major plus for reproducibility and adoption by the community.
    * **Addressing limitations of existing approaches:** Effectively addresses the problem of non-differentiable steps in related methods as well as removes the need to rely on CAD models or shape priors as the main drivers for pose estimation, which are often difficult and time-consuming to prepare.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on DINOv2:** While claiming to be shape prior-free, the method relies heavily on the DINOv2 large vision model. While not a CAD model *per se*, DINOv2 encodes significant prior knowledge about object shapes and scene structure. This dependence limits the claim of being completely "prior-free." The authors should acknowledge the role of DINOv2 as a learned implicit prior more explicitly.
    * **Computational Cost:** Diffusion models are generally computationally expensive, particularly during inference.  While the paper mentions achieving a speed of 26.3 frames per second, more detailed benchmarking of runtime performance and resource requirements on a variety of hardware configurations would be useful.
    * **Limited Generalization Evaluation:** While the authors perform generalization tests on the WILD6D dataset, a more comprehensive exploration of generalization to unseen object categories or drastically different environments would strengthen the paper.

* **Potential Influence:**  The paper has the potential to influence research in monocular pose estimation by demonstrating the feasibility of using diffusion models conditioned on features extracted from pre-trained large vision models to perform category-level pose estimation without explicit shape priors. Other researchers could build upon this approach, for example, by exploring alternative conditioning mechanisms or denoiser architectures. It's likely that future work will attempt to further reduce the reliance on pre-trained models like DINOv2.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.  Here's why:

*   The paper addresses a relevant problem with a novel approach.
*   The experimental results are strong and support the claims.
*   The open-source code will facilitate further research.
*   However, the work is not a complete paradigm shift, but rather an incremental improvement building on existing methods.
*   The reliance on DINOv2 limits the claim of being shape-prior-free. While DINOv2 does not provide a *direct* shape representation (e.g., a mesh), it is trained on a massive dataset, which allows it to encode a *general* prior about the appearance and geometry of objects. The authors fail to explicitly discuss this aspect. The reliance on external, large-scale pre-training undermines the paper's novelty claims to some degree.
*   More work is needed to fully assess the generalization capabilities and practical applicability of the method.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Anchor Token Matching: Implicit Structure Locking for Training-free AR Image Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10434v1)
- **Authors**: Taihang Hu, Linxuan Li, Kai Wang, Yaxing Wang, Jian Yang, Ming-Ming Cheng
- **Abstract**: Text-to-image generation has seen groundbreaking advancements with diffusion models, enabling high-fidelity synthesis and precise image editing through cross-attention manipulation. Recently, autoregressive (AR) models have re-emerged as powerful alternatives, leveraging next-token generation to match diffusion models. However, existing editing techniques designed for diffusion models fail to translate directly to AR models due to fundamental differences in structural control. Specifically, AR models suffer from spatial poverty of attention maps and sequential accumulation of structural errors during image editing, which disrupt object layouts and global consistency. In this work, we introduce Implicit Structure Locking (ISLock), the first training-free editing strategy for AR visual models. Rather than relying on explicit attention manipulation or fine-tuning, ISLock preserves structural blueprints by dynamically aligning self-attention patterns with reference images through the Anchor Token Matching (ATM) protocol. By implicitly enforcing structural consistency in latent space, our method ISLock enables structure-aware editing while maintaining generative autonomy. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ISLock achieves high-quality, structure-consistent edits without additional training and is superior or comparable to conventional editing techniques. Our findings pioneer the way for efficient and flexible AR-based image editing, further bridging the performance gap between diffusion and autoregressive generative models. The code will be publicly available at https://github.com/hutaiHang/ATM
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Implicit Structure Locking (ISLock), a novel training-free approach for image editing using autoregressive (AR) models. The key idea is to preserve structural integrity during editing by dynamically aligning self-attention patterns of the edited image with those of a reference image, achieved through Anchor Token Matching (ATM). This avoids the drawbacks of directly injecting attention maps from diffusion model techniques, which can disrupt the inherent attention dynamics and introduce inconsistencies in AR models. ISLock selects the most structurally similar token during autoregressive decoding, implicitly guiding the model to maintain the original image's structure while allowing semantic edits.  The method is evaluated across various editing tasks (object replacement, addition, etc.) and compared with both diffusion-based and AR-based editing techniques. Results demonstrate that ISLock achieves high-quality, structure-consistent edits without requiring additional training, outperforming or matching existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper tackles a significant problem: the lack of effective, training-free image editing methods for autoregressive models. The core idea of ISLock and ATM is innovative in the context of AR models. Unlike diffusion models that rely on direct cross-attention manipulation, the implicit structure locking by matching attention patterns is a novel approach. The introduction of the dynamic window mechanism to balance structure preservation and generative autonomy is a further refinement that adds to the novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of AR-based image generation and editing. By developing a zero-shot editing technique, it lowers the barrier to entry for controlling AR models, making them more accessible and adaptable. The extensive experiments on the PIE-Bench dataset provide solid evidence of ISLock's effectiveness and superiority over existing techniques. The insights into the attention mechanisms of AR models and their structural control are valuable contributions to the broader understanding of these models. The method effectively bridges the gap between diffusion and autoregressive models in image editing.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The method is training-free, providing significant practical advantages in terms of resource requirements.
    *   The idea of implicit structure locking is well-motivated and elegantly executed.
    *   The experimental results are comprehensive and demonstrate the effectiveness of ISLock across diverse editing tasks.
    *   The analysis of attention mechanisms in AR models is insightful and contributes to a better understanding of these models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While results are generally strong, there are some minor visual artifacts in several cases. The algorithm relies on the quality of the input image generated from the original prompt, so poor prompt engineering will translate into sub-optimal edit results.
    *   The complexity of the ATM process introduces computational overhead compared to simple prompt manipulation. The computational cost in a practical setting may outweigh the performance.
    *   The reliance on a discrete codebook representation might limit the fine-grained control over image details compared to pixel-level manipulations in diffusion models.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in several ways:
    *   It establishes a new direction for image editing in AR models, moving away from explicit attention manipulation toward implicit structural guidance.
    *   It stimulates further investigation into the attention mechanisms and structural control in AR models.
    *   It provides a practical and effective zero-shot editing technique for AR models, making them more competitive with diffusion models.
    *   It may facilitate the development of more controllable and interpretable AR models.

**Justification:**

Despite the minor limitations, the paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field. The ISLock approach is well-motivated, technically sound, and experimentally validated. The paper addresses a critical gap in AR-based image editing, providing a training-free solution that achieves impressive results. The insights into AR model attention are valuable and can guide future research. The paper lays the groundwork for future work in controlled generation from AR visual generative models.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Multimodal Long Video Modeling Based on Temporal Dynamic Context
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10443v1)
- **Authors**: Haoran Hao, Jiaming Han, Yiyuan Zhang, Xiangyu Yue
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in Large Language Models (LLMs) have led to significant breakthroughs in video understanding. However, existing models still struggle with long video processing due to the context length constraint of LLMs and the vast amount of information within the video. Although some recent methods are designed for long video understanding, they often lose crucial information during token compression and struggle with additional modality like audio. In this work, we propose a dynamic long video encoding method utilizing the temporal relationship between frames, named Temporal Dynamic Context (TDC). Firstly, we segment the video into semantically consistent scenes based on inter-frame similarities, then encode each frame into tokens using visual-audio encoders. Secondly, we propose a novel temporal context compressor to reduce the number of tokens within each segment. Specifically, we employ a query-based Transformer to aggregate video, audio, and instruction text tokens into a limited set of temporal context tokens. Finally, we feed the static frame tokens and the temporal context tokens into the LLM for video understanding. Furthermore, to handle extremely long videos, we propose a training-free chain-of-thought strategy that progressively extracts answers from multiple video segments. These intermediate answers serve as part of the reasoning process and contribute to the final answer. We conduct extensive experiments on general video understanding and audio-video understanding benchmarks, where our method demonstrates strong performance. The code and models are available at https://github.com/Hoar012/TDC-Video.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Multimodal Long Video Modeling Based on Temporal Dynamic Context":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenges of long video understanding in large language models (LLMs), focusing on context length limitations and the integration of multiple modalities (specifically audio and video).  The authors propose a method called Temporal Dynamic Context (TDC). It involves: (1) segmenting the video into semantically consistent scenes using inter-frame similarity, (2) encoding each scene using both visual and audio encoders, retaining static keyframe features and compressing subsequent frames into temporal context tokens using a query-based Transformer (Q-Former), and (3) employing a training-free chain-of-thought strategy called Long Video Chain-of-Thought (LVCoT) for extremely long videos, processing them in segments and combining intermediate answers.  The authors train models of varying sizes using a multi-stage training process, including vision-language alignment, video instruction tuning, and audio-video instruction tuning. The evaluation is done on a range of video benchmarks (MVBench, PerceptionTest, etc.) and audio-video question answering benchmarks (Music-QA, AVSD).  The method demonstrates strong performance compared to existing approaches.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper has several aspects contributing to novelty, though the individual components aren't entirely new. The combination of scene segmentation based on visual consistency, the query-based Q-Former compression integrating both audio and visual information (TDC), and the training-free LVCoT is a novel and effective approach. Other works have explored similar techniques like keyframe selection, inter-frame redundancies compression, visual similarity based segmentation and LVCoT (mostly used for short videos), but the combination and specific implementation here demonstrates tangible improvements. The Q-former part, using average pooling on static features also appears to be novel.

*   **Significance:**  The paper is significant because it tackles the important problem of long video understanding, a major bottleneck in applying LLMs to real-world video data. While some prior works have addressed video understanding, they often suffer from information loss during compression or poor multimodal integration.  The proposed TDC method effectively addresses both of these issues. Its strong performance across various benchmarks suggests its potential to advance the field. The use of a training-free chain-of-thought approach (LVCoT) is also significant as it enhances the capabilities of existing MLLMs without requiring extensive retraining. The modularity of the solution is appealing, allowing components to be adapted and integrated into other architectures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The TDC method combines several techniques in a novel way, leading to effective long video modeling.
    *   Integration of audio and visual modalities within the TDC framework is well-motivated and demonstrated.
    *   The LVCoT strategy offers a practical solution for handling extremely long videos without additional training.
    *   Comprehensive experiments on diverse benchmarks showcase the method's strong performance.
    *   The paper is clearly written and well-structured, making it easy to understand the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The Q-Former part benefits a bit more detail. The experiments section could expand a bit to explore the impact of different hyperparameters on segment size, # of context tokens, and Q-Former configurations.
    *   Although LVCoT is training-free, its effectiveness is dependent on the reasoning ability of the underlying LLM. The paper mentions this limitation, but it could be explored further.
    *   While the experimental results are strong, the paper would benefit from ablations on even more of the individual components of TDC to quantify their specific contributions.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper has a good potential to influence the field by offering an effective and relatively efficient approach to long video understanding with good multimodal integration. The TDC and LVCoT methods can be adopted or extended by other researchers and practitioners. The insights into the importance of static frame features, temporal dynamics, and chain-of-thought reasoning can guide future research directions in this area.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper demonstrates a significant advancement in multimodal long video understanding, successfully combining existing techniques in a novel and effective way (TDC & LVCoT). The experimental results are compelling, and the paper is well-written. While the individual components are not completely groundbreaking, the integrated system offers tangible improvements over existing methods. The potential impact on the field is promising due to the effective combination of techniques and clear experimental validation. Room for improvement lies primarily in deeper ablations and a more thorough analysis of the impact of the LLM's reasoning capability on the efficacy of the LVCoT method, hence it did not score higher. But it has a clear potential impact in the long video analysis field.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### M1: Towards Scalable Test-Time Compute with Mamba Reasoning Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10449v1)
- **Authors**: Junxiong Wang, Wen-Ding Li, Daniele Paliotta, Daniel Ritter, Alexander M. Rush, Tri Dao
- **Abstract**: Effective reasoning is crucial to solving complex mathematical problems. Recent large language models (LLMs) have boosted performance by scaling test-time computation through long chain-of-thought reasoning. However, transformer-based models are inherently limited in extending context length due to their quadratic computational complexity and linear memory requirements. In this paper, we introduce a novel hybrid linear RNN reasoning model, M1, built on the Mamba architecture, which allows memory-efficient inference. Our approach leverages a distillation process from existing reasoning models and is further enhanced through RL training. Experimental results on the AIME and MATH benchmarks show that M1 not only outperforms previous linear RNN models but also matches the performance of state-of-the-art Deepseek R1 distilled reasoning models at a similar scale. We also compare our generation speed with a highly performant general purpose inference engine, vLLM, and observe more than a 3x speedup compared to a same size transformer. With throughput speedup, we are able to achieve higher accuracy compared to DeepSeek R1 distilled transformer reasoning models under a fixed generation time budget using self-consistency voting. Overall, we introduce a hybrid Mamba reasoning model and provide a more effective approach to scaling test-time generation using self-consistency or long chain of thought reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces M1, a novel hybrid linear Recurrent Neural Network (RNN) reasoning model based on the Mamba architecture. M1 aims to overcome the limitations of transformer-based models in handling long sequences and large batch sizes due to their quadratic computational complexity and linear memory requirements. The authors employ a multi-stage training process involving distillation from existing reasoning models, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on mathematical datasets, and reinforcement learning (RL) to enhance reasoning capabilities. Experimental results on the AIME and MATH benchmarks demonstrate that M1 outperforms previous linear RNN models and matches the performance of state-of-the-art DeepSeek R1 distilled reasoning models at a similar scale, while also achieving a significant throughput speedup using self-consistency voting.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in the design and implementation of a hybrid Mamba-based reasoning model (M1) and the demonstration of its effectiveness through a rigorous training and evaluation pipeline. Prior works explored Mamba architectures, distillation techniques, and RL for reasoning tasks separately. The key innovation is the combination of these techniques tailored to the Mamba architecture for mathematical reasoning. It's an incremental improvement, not a fundamentally new paradigm. The use of existing frameworks (Axolotl and VeRL) and established distillation and RL techniques (GRPO) reduces the novelty somewhat. However, the specific adaptation and fine-tuning of these methods for the Mamba architecture in the context of mathematical reasoning contributes a valuable engineering effort.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this paper stems from its potential to provide a more efficient and scalable alternative to transformer-based models for reasoning tasks. The demonstrated 3x speedup compared to similarly-sized transformers, combined with comparable accuracy on challenging benchmarks, highlights the practical benefits of M1. This could significantly reduce the computational cost of inference, enabling larger-scale deployments and more extensive exploration of test-time compute techniques (e.g., self-consistency). The comparison of different training stages (distillation, SFT, RL) is valuable for understanding the contributions of each component. It provides insights into how to best transfer reasoning capabilities from large transformer models to smaller, more efficient RNN architectures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear and well-structured presentation.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on established benchmarks.
    *   Detailed description of the training pipeline and hyperparameter settings.
    *   Analysis of the impact of different training stages and sequence lengths.
    *   Demonstrated speedup in inference compared to transformer models.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The core techniques (distillation, SFT, RL, Mamba architecture) are not fundamentally new.
    *   Reliance on existing frameworks (Axolotl and VeRL) reduces the technical contribution.
    *   The experimental results, while promising, could be strengthened by comparisons to other state-of-the-art linear RNN reasoning models (if available).
    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the trade-offs between speed, accuracy, and memory usage.
    *   While the authors discuss improvements in RL training with longer sequences, no further explanations or theoretical motivations are provided.
    *   Direct distillation results from reasoning models are poor, but this result might lack further in-depth analysis or theoretical validation.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development of more efficient and scalable reasoning models. The M1 architecture and training pipeline provide a valuable blueprint for researchers interested in exploring hybrid RNN alternatives to transformers. The findings on test-time compute scaling could also motivate further investigation into the optimal allocation of computational resources during inference.

**Overall:**

While the paper builds upon existing techniques, the engineering effort of creating a high-performing Mamba-based reasoning model, demonstrating its efficiency, and analyzing its performance characteristics contributes a valuable addition to the field. The work has practical relevance due to the potential for cost reduction in inference, and it demonstrates the feasibility of using RNNs for complex tasks like mathematical reasoning.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** A score of 7 reflects the paper's incremental but significant contribution. The work isn't groundbreaking in terms of introducing fundamentally new architectures or algorithms, but the clever combination of known techniques into M1 and the thorough experimental analysis provides valuable insights. The potential for a significant speedup in inference, with competitive accuracy, justifies a score above average. The paper also sets a solid baseline for future research in this area and offers an opportunity for follow-up work to analyze the potential benefits of the model in the context of RL and test-time compute.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Integrating Vision and Location with Transformers: A Multimodal Deep Learning Framework for Medical Wound Analysis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10452v1)
- **Authors**: Ramin Mousa, Hadis Taherinia, Khabiba Abdiyeva, Amir Ali Bengari, Mohammadmahdi Vahediahmar
- **Abstract**: Effective recognition of acute and difficult-to-heal wounds is a necessary step in wound diagnosis. An efficient classification model can help wound specialists classify wound types with less financial and time costs and also help in deciding on the optimal treatment method. Traditional machine learning models suffer from feature selection and are usually cumbersome models for accurate recognition. Recently, deep learning (DL) has emerged as a powerful tool in wound diagnosis. Although DL seems promising for wound type recognition, there is still a large scope for improving the efficiency and accuracy of the model. In this study, a DL-based multimodal classifier was developed using wound images and their corresponding locations to classify them into multiple classes, including diabetic, pressure, surgical, and venous ulcers. A body map was also created to provide location data, which can help wound specialists label wound locations more effectively. The model uses a Vision Transformer to extract hierarchical features from input images, a Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT) layer to capture low and high frequency components, and a Transformer to extract spatial features. The number of neurons and weight vector optimization were performed using three swarm-based optimization techniques (Monster Gorilla Toner (MGTO), Improved Gray Wolf Optimization (IGWO), and Fox Optimization Algorithm). The evaluation results show that weight vector optimization using optimization algorithms can increase diagnostic accuracy and make it a very effective approach for wound detection. In the classification using the original body map, the proposed model was able to achieve an accuracy of 0.8123 using image data and an accuracy of 0.8007 using a combination of image data and wound location. Also, the accuracy of the model in combination with the optimization models varied from 0.7801 to 0.8342.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a multimodal deep learning framework for medical wound analysis, aiming to improve the accuracy and reliability of wound classification. It integrates wound images with their anatomical location information using a Vision Transformer (ViT) to extract visual features, a Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT) layer to capture frequency components, and a Transformer to extract spatial features from the location data (represented as a body map). The authors employ three swarm-based optimization techniques (MGTO, IGWO, FOX) to optimize the network's parameters. The results show that incorporating location data improves classification accuracy and that using optimization algorithms further enhances the performance of the model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Multimodal Approach:** Integrating wound images with anatomical location is a significant strength. Prior works indicate improved performance with such fusion, and this paper reinforces that finding.
    *   **Use of Vision Transformers (ViTs):** Exploiting ViTs for wound image analysis is a relatively newer approach, and the authors appropriately justify its suitability for capturing global dependencies and multi-scale features relevant in wound characteristics.
    *   **Wavelet Integration:** The use of a DWT layer to explicitly capture frequency components adds another layer of feature extraction sophistication, potentially improving robustness to noise and variability in wound images.
    *   **Optimization Techniques:** The use of swarm-based optimization algorithms to tune the network parameters is a good practice. These algorithms can potentially help find better parameter configurations compared to standard gradient-based methods.
    *   **Comparisons:** The authors present extensive comparison results with different combinations of models and data modalities.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the paper combines several techniques (ViTs, DWT, Transformers, location information, swarm optimization), the novelty feels incremental rather than revolutionary. Each of these components has been explored in other contexts. The primary contribution lies in assembling them for this specific application.
    *   **Dataset Description:**  While the paper mentions the AZH dataset, more details on the dataset's properties, demographics, and potential biases are warranted.  A clear breakdown of samples per wound type would improve the assessment of results.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Explainability:** Although explainability is briefly mentioned in the introduction as a motivator, the paper does not delve into concrete explainability techniques or analyze the model's decision-making process.
    *   **Optimization Results:** Although optimization algorithms are used the increase in accuracy isnt drastic.
    *   **Body map and location:** The dataset contained a static body map with 484 locations. The creation of the body map seems a bit arbitrary.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Practical Application:** Wound analysis is a clinically relevant problem, and improving the accuracy of wound classification can have tangible benefits for patient care.
    *   **Methodological Contribution:** The paper provides a detailed example of how to integrate multiple deep learning techniques and data modalities for a medical image analysis task. This can serve as a useful template for other researchers working on similar problems.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper addresses a significant problem and demonstrates a functional multimodal deep learning framework with empirical improvements, its novelty isn't groundbreaking. The core techniques (ViTs, Transformers, location data) are established, and the contribution is mainly in their application and combination for wound analysis. The limited depth of the explainability analysis and the lack of thorough discussion on the AZH dataset further detract from its impact. The increase in accuracy provided by the optimization algorithms isnt drastic. Therefore, a score of 6 is appropriate.
