To facilitate the benchmarking of images as light sources, we introduce a novel dataset of calibrated and unsaturated HDR to evaluate images as light sources. We assess our method using a combination of this novel dataset and an existing dataset from the literature. The code to reproduce our method will be available upon acceptance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the GASLIGHT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GASLIGHT, a novel framework for representing and capturing spatially varying lighting in HDR scenes from a set of regular, low-dynamic-range (LDR) images. GASLIGHT leverages HDR Gaussian Splats to model 3D lighting. The method comprises two key stages: (1) a diffusion model-based LDR-to-HDR upsampling network that plausibly and accurately enhances the dynamic range of the input images, paying special attention to extrapolating the intensity of bright light sources, and (2) the use of Gaussian Splats to create an explicit 3D HDR lighting representation, enabling effects like near-field lighting and high-frequency reflections. The paper also introduces a new dataset of calibrated HDR images to serve as light sources for benchmarking, along with comparisons against existing methods on both novel and existing datasets. The method aims to produce a lighting representation suitable for insertion of virtual objects in a scene.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes several contributions that collectively enhance the state of the art:
    *   **HDR Gaussian Splats:** The use of Gaussian Splats to explicitly represent HDR lighting is novel and potentially impactful. It combines the advantages of both parametric (point lights) and non-parametric (environment maps) approaches.
    *   **Diffusion Model-based HDR Upsampling:** While diffusion models have been used for inverse tone mapping, the paper presents a specific network design that focuses on light source intensity extrapolation rather than just general dynamic range enhancement. The use of a recursive refinement process to progressively expand the HDR range seems efficient.
    *   **Dataset for Lighting Benchmarking:** The novel dataset of calibrated unsaturated HDR images to benchmark *light source reproduction* has a lasting benefit, as the existing datasets focus more on re-tonemapping than accurate light source estimation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses important limitations of existing lighting estimation methods. Current methods struggle to accurately capture high-frequency, spatially varying HDR lighting with strong cast shadows from regular images. GASLIGHT tackles these limitations, and its ability to readily integrate within existing rendering pipelines and enable virtual object insertion makes it practical and valuable.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Methodology:** The paper clearly describes the two-stage process, including details of the network architecture and training procedure.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The method is evaluated on multiple datasets, demonstrating its superior performance on bright light source intensity prediction and compared against existing methods for virtual object insertion.
    *   **Practical Application:** The results on virtual object insertion are compelling, showing the potential of GASLIGHT to create realistic composite scenes.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Code availability upon acceptance is a huge plus for transparency and allows other researchers to build upon this work.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of 3D Lighting Effects:** While using gaussian splats helps, the paper acknowledges that complex lighting effects like interreflections and scattering are not well-represented. This can be a limitation when rendering complex scenes.
    *   **Multi-View Consistency:** The authors explicitly state that multi-view consistency is not enforced during HDR upsampling, potentially leading to view-dependent artifacts. Although spherical harmonics and the nature of gaussian splatting might mitigate that somewhat, it should be explored in detail.
    *   **Scalability of InstantSplat:** The reliance on InstantSplat may limit the performance of the proposed technique in large scale scenes and the authors acknowledge that. The community is rapidly improving the scalability of gaussian splatting though so this may not be a major issue.
    *   **Dataset Limited to Static Scenes:** The new dataset acquired in this study uses static scenes. A dataset with dynamic lighting could be a valuable direction for future work.

*   **Impact:** This paper can influence the field by:
    *   **Providing a new way to represent HDR lighting:** The work will encourage further research into explicit representations of lighting using Gaussian Splats.
    *   **Offering a benchmark for light source estimation:** The release of the calibrated HDR dataset will standardize evaluations in this area.
    *   **Facilitating realistic virtual object insertion:** The framework provides a practical approach for compositing virtual objects into real scenes with accurate lighting effects.

The combination of a robust diffusion model for HDR estimation with the flexibility of Gaussian Splats creates a powerful framework. The paper introduces new methods and data and also clearly identifies its limitations and directions for future work.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**
The paper presents a novel and practical approach to HDR lighting capture and representation, addressing limitations of existing methods and releasing a valuable new dataset. The potential impact of the work on virtual object insertion and other related applications is significant. The score is not higher due to limitations in representing complex lighting effects, the lack of explicit multi-view consistency and scalability concerns with the instant splatting implementation. Future work addressing these limitations could increase the score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### CSPLADE: Learned Sparse Retrieval with Causal Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10816v1)
- **Authors**: Zhichao Xu, Aosong Feng, Yijun Tian, Haibo Ding, Lin Leee Cheong
- **Abstract**: In recent years, dense retrieval has been the focus of information retrieval (IR) research. While effective, dense retrieval produces uninterpretable dense vectors, and suffers from the drawback of large index size. Learned sparse retrieval (LSR) has emerged as promising alternative, achieving competitive retrieval performance while also being able to leverage the classical inverted index data structure for efficient retrieval. However, limited works have explored scaling LSR beyond BERT scale. In this work, we identify two challenges in training large language models (LLM) for LSR: (1) training instability during the early stage of contrastive training; (2) suboptimal performance due to pre-trained LLM's unidirectional attention. To address these challenges, we propose two corresponding techniques: (1) a lightweight adaptation training phase to eliminate training instability; (2) two model variants to enable bidirectional information. With these techniques, we are able to train LSR models with 8B scale LLM, and achieve competitive retrieval performance with reduced index size. Furthermore, we are among the first to analyze the performance-efficiency tradeoff of LLM-based LSR model through the lens of model quantization. Our findings provide insights into adapting LLMs for efficient retrieval modeling.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CSPLADE: Learned Sparse Retrieval with Causal Language Models":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of scaling Learned Sparse Retrieval (LSR) to large language models (LLMs).  The authors identify two key issues when training LLMs for LSR: (1) training instability (specifically, dying ReLU) early in contrastive learning and (2) suboptimal performance due to LLMs' unidirectional attention. To tackle these problems, they propose two techniques: (1) a lightweight adaptation training phase with causal language modeling and log-saturation loss to improve training stability, and (2) two model variants (echo embedding and direct bidirectional attention) to enable bidirectional information flow within the LLM. They name their method CAUSAL SPLADE (CSPLADE) and demonstrate competitive retrieval performance with 8B parameter LLMs and reduced index size. Furthermore, the paper investigates the performance-efficiency tradeoff using model quantization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

*   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper directly targets a significant bottleneck in applying LLMs to retrieval: scaling LSR. While dense retrieval with LLMs is gaining traction, LSR offers advantages in interpretability and potentially smaller index sizes. Addressing the challenges of scaling LSR to LLMs is therefore well motivated.
*   **Identifies Specific Challenges:** The paper's identification of training instability (dying ReLU) and unidirectional attention issues in LLM-based LSR is a valuable contribution. This provides concrete problems for the community to address.
*   **Proposes Reasonable Solutions:**  The proposed adaptation phase tackles the dying ReLU problem in a straightforward and effective manner by pre-adapting LLMs with causal language modeling and log-saturation loss. The echo embedding and direct bidirectional attention for unidirectional LMs are modifications inspired by previous work that show improvement.
*   **Quantization Analysis:** The analysis of quantization trade-offs is a practical and valuable addition, given the cost of LLM inference.

**Significance:**

*   **Competitive Performance:** The paper demonstrates competitive results, achieving strong retrieval performance with an 8B parameter LLM and a significantly reduced index size compared to dense methods. This strengthens the case for LSR as a viable alternative to dense retrieval with LLMs.
*   **Practical Insights:** The quantization experiments provide practical insights into the efficiency of different quantization methods, which can guide practitioners in deploying LLM-based LSR models.
*   **Future Research Directions:** The paper opens avenues for future research, such as exploring better pre-training methods, architecture modifications, and more advanced training strategies.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Contribution:** The solutions, while effective, are somewhat incremental. The adaptation phase, echo embedding, and bidirectional attention techniques are inspired by existing methods in other contexts (though their application to LLM-based LSR is novel).
*   **Limited Scope:** The paper focuses specifically on SPLADE and LLAMA-3 family models. It could be strengthened by investigating other LSR formulations or LLMs.
*   **Experimental Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation lacks in-depth analysis. For example, additional ablation studies on the contributions of individual components would enhance understanding. A more granular analysis of per-dataset performance on BEIR would also be beneficial. Also, the scaling and comparisons against sparse retrievers that leverage cross-encoders (especially for BEIR) is limited.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a solid contribution to the field by addressing a specific challenge in applying LLMs to LSR. The identification of the dying ReLU and unidirectional attention issues is insightful, and the proposed solutions offer practical improvements. While the techniques themselves are somewhat incremental, their application to the LLM-based LSR setting is novel and provides valuable empirical results and practical insights. The investigation of quantization trade-offs further enhances the paper's practical relevance.

However, the paper's novelty is limited, as the core techniques build upon existing methods. The experimental evaluation could be more in-depth with more detailed ablation studies and comparisons. Given these strengths and weaknesses, I believe a score in the upper-middle range is appropriate.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.IR
- **Score**: 7/10

### IlluSign: Illustrating Sign Language Videos by Leveraging the Attention Mechanism
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10822v1)
- **Authors**: Janna Bruner, Amit Moryossef, Lior Wolf
- **Abstract**: Sign languages are dynamic visual languages that involve hand gestures, in combination with non manual elements such as facial expressions. While video recordings of sign language are commonly used for education and documentation, the dynamic nature of signs can make it challenging to study them in detail, especially for new learners and educators. This work aims to convert sign language video footage into static illustrations, which serve as an additional educational resource to complement video content. This process is usually done by an artist, and is therefore quite costly. We propose a method that illustrates sign language videos by leveraging generative models' ability to understand both the semantic and geometric aspects of images. Our approach focuses on transferring a sketch like illustration style to video footage of sign language, combining the start and end frames of a sign into a single illustration, and using arrows to highlight the hand's direction and motion. While many style transfer methods address domain adaptation at varying levels of abstraction, applying a sketch like style to sign languages, especially for hand gestures and facial expressions, poses a significant challenge. To tackle this, we intervene in the denoising process of a diffusion model, injecting style as keys and values into high resolution attention layers, and fusing geometric information from the image and edges as queries. For the final illustration, we use the attention mechanism to combine the attention weights from both the start and end illustrations, resulting in a soft combination. Our method offers a cost effective solution for generating sign language illustrations at inference time, addressing the lack of such resources in educational materials.
- **Summary**: **Concise Summary:** The paper titled "IlluSign: Illustrating Sign Language Videos by Leveraging the Attention Mechanism" proposes an innovative method to illustrate sign language videos, transforming dynamic visual language into static illustrations for educational purposes. The authors aim to address the challenges of studying sign languages, which comprise hand gestures and facial expressions, through a more cost-effective approach compared to traditional artistry. Leveraging generative models and the attention mechanism, the method focuses on infusing a sketch-like style into sign language video frames by merging the initial and terminal frames of a sign along with directional cues using arrows. This approach operates through a modified diffusion model that employs attention layers to refine the fusion of geometric information, ultimately producing clear and informative illustrations. The authors emphasize that their method serves as a valuable educational resource, filling a critical gap in sign language learning materials.  **Evaluation and Rationale:** The novelty of this paper is evident in its intersection of generative models, attention mechanisms, and sign language, areas that are typically not combinative within existing literature. The method proposes a fresh approach to facilitate the understanding of sign language by visual learners, which is quite significant, considering the growing need for inclusive educational tools. The use of cost-effective illustrations as opposed to traditional artistic methods also represents a practical advancement that could democratize access to sign language education. One of the key strengths of the paper is its methodological approach—intervening in the diffusion model to produce engaging visual representations while efficiently using computational resources. Additionally, the emphasis on combining frames and highlighting motion showcases an insightful understanding of the temporal dynamics of sign language. However, the paper could be further strengthened by an empirical evaluation demonstrating the effectiveness of these illustrations in the educational process. While the theoretical underpinnings are robust, actual user studies or case applications would provide stronger validation of the methodology. Furthermore, the paper would benefit from a comparative analysis with existing sign language teaching aids to highlight improvements distinctly enabled by this technique. The paper's contribution to the field, while significant, hinges on its practical application and wider adoption. Although the approach is innovative, without extensive studies showcasing its direct impact on learning outcomes, its transformative potential remains somewhat theoretical. In summary, the paper presents a valuable advance in the educational resources for sign languages, yet its full impact would be ascertained through future empirical studies. Given these considerations, I assign the paper the following score: **Score: 7**  This score reflects its solid methodological foundation and relevance in addressing a significant gap in educational resources, while also considering the need for more empirical evidence to substantiate its practical benefits.
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### CLASH: Evaluating Language Models on Judging High-Stakes Dilemmas from Multiple Perspectives
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10823v1)
- **Authors**: Ayoung Lee, Ryan Sungmo Kwon, Peter Railton, Lu Wang
- **Abstract**: Navigating high-stakes dilemmas involving conflicting values is challenging even for humans, let alone for AI. Yet prior work in evaluating the reasoning capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in such situations has been limited to everyday scenarios. To close this gap, this work first introduces CLASH (Character perspective-based LLM Assessments in Situations with High-stakes), a meticulously curated dataset consisting of 345 high-impact dilemmas along with 3,795 individual perspectives of diverse values. In particular, we design CLASH in a way to support the study of critical aspects of value-based decision-making processes which are missing from prior work, including understanding decision ambivalence and psychological discomfort as well as capturing the temporal shifts of values in characters' perspectives. By benchmarking 10 open and closed frontier models, we uncover several key findings. (1) Even the strongest models, such as GPT-4o and Claude-Sonnet, achieve less than 50% accuracy in identifying situations where the decision should be ambivalent, while they perform significantly better in clear-cut scenarios. (2) While LLMs reasonably predict psychological discomfort as marked by human, they inadequately comprehend perspectives involving value shifts, indicating a need for LLMs to reason over complex values. (3) Our experiments also reveal a significant correlation between LLMs' value preferences and their steerability towards a given value. (4) Finally, LLMs exhibit greater steerability when engaged in value reasoning from a third-party perspective, compared to a first-person setup, though certain value pairs benefit uniquely from the first-person framing.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces CLASH, a new dataset designed to evaluate the ability of large language models (LLMs) to make judgments in high-stakes dilemmas, considering multiple perspectives and nuances in values. The dataset consists of 345 human-written, long-form dilemmas, each enriched with diverse value perspectives presented as character narratives. The authors use CLASH to investigate three important aspects of value reasoning: decision ambivalence, psychological discomfort, and the understanding of value shifts. They benchmarked ten open and closed frontier models and analyzed the models' steerability based on value pairs.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in the creation of the CLASH dataset. Existing datasets often focus on everyday scenarios and lack the complexity and depth of the high-stakes dilemmas found in CLASH. Moreover, prior datasets typically use short situation descriptions and often generate situations synthetically, which is different from the long-form, human-written dilemmas in CLASH. The inclusion of detailed character descriptions representing diverse value perspectives is another novel aspect, moving beyond simple value lists or demographic information. The paper’s exploration of previously unexplored aspects of value reasoning such as decision ambivalence, psychological discomfort, and dynamic value shifts in LLMs also provides novelty.

*   **Significance:** Evaluating LLMs in value-sensitive applications is crucial, especially as these models are increasingly deployed in domains like healthcare, law, and finance. The paper’s findings that LLMs struggle with decision ambivalence and value shifts highlight limitations that could have significant consequences in real-world scenarios. The analysis of steerability based on competing value pairs offers practical insights for aligning AI with complex and pluralistic human values. Furthermore, the exploration of question framing strategies (first-person vs. third-person perspectives) can inform the design of more effective human-AI interactions. However, the general steerability framework is not very novel and mainly leverages pre-existing value mapping from DailyDilemmas.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The CLASH dataset fills a gap in existing resources for evaluating value-based reasoning in LLMs.
    *   The inclusion of character narratives provides rich contextual information that enhances the realism and complexity of the evaluation.
    *   The analysis of decision ambivalence, psychological discomfort, and value shifts offers valuable insights into the limitations of current LLMs.
    *   The investigation of steerability based on competing value pairs provides practical implications for aligning AI with human values.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The analysis of the impact of question framing, while interesting, could be more rigorously investigated with a larger sample size.
    *   Although the paper compares numerous models, the analysis is mainly focused on accuracy, while a more in-depth exploration of the reasoning behind the models' decisions could provide deeper insights. Error analysis could be more robust in linking errors back to specific components of the model architecture.
    *   Although the high-stakes scenarios increase novelty, certain design choices like directly utilizing mappings from existing datasets to steer LLMs using competing value pairs, while helpful, does reduce novelty.

*   **Potential Influence:** The CLASH dataset has the potential to become a valuable resource for researchers working on value alignment and ethical AI. The paper's findings could inform the development of new techniques for improving LLMs' reasoning capabilities in complex, value-laden situations. By highlighting the limitations of current models, the paper can stimulate research into more robust and nuanced approaches to value alignment.

**Justification for Score:** The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing a novel dataset and exploring important aspects of value reasoning in LLMs. However, some of the analytical methods and findings, particularly in the steerability section, are somewhat incremental. A more in-depth analysis of error cases, and more thorough analysis of other approaches to question framing in LLMs would further strengthen the paper.

**Score: 7**
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### OmniVDiff: Omni Controllable Video Diffusion for Generation and Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10825v1)
- **Authors**: Dianbing Xi, Jiepeng Wang, Yuanzhi Liang, Xi Qiu, Yuchi Huo, Rui Wang, Chi Zhang, Xuelong Li
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we propose a novel framework for controllable video diffusion, OmniVDiff, aiming to synthesize and comprehend multiple video visual content in a single diffusion model. To achieve this, OmniVDiff treats all video visual modalities in the color space to learn a joint distribution, while employing an adaptive control strategy that dynamically adjusts the role of each visual modality during the diffusion process, either as a generation modality or a conditioning modality. This allows flexible manipulation of each modality's role, enabling support for a wide range of tasks. Consequently, our model supports three key functionalities: (1) Text-conditioned video generation: multi-modal visual video sequences (i.e., rgb, depth, canny, segmentaion) are generated based on the text conditions in one diffusion process; (2) Video understanding: OmniVDiff can estimate the depth, canny map, and semantic segmentation across the input rgb frames while ensuring coherence with the rgb input; and (3) X-conditioned video generation: OmniVDiff generates videos conditioned on fine-grained attributes (e.g., depth maps or segmentation maps). By integrating these diverse tasks into a unified video diffusion framework, OmniVDiff enhances the flexibility and scalability for controllable video diffusion, making it an effective tool for a variety of downstream applications, such as video-to-video translation. Extensive experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of our approach, highlighting its potential for various video-related applications.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "OmniVDiff: Omni Controllable Video Diffusion for Generation and Understanding".

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OmniVDiff, a novel video diffusion framework designed for controllable video generation and understanding. Unlike existing approaches that often require task-specific fine-tuning or rely on external expert models, OmniVDiff aims to unify multiple video-related tasks within a single diffusion model. It achieves this by treating different video modalities (e.g., RGB, depth, segmentation, canny edges) in the color space, learning a joint distribution.  An adaptive control strategy is employed to dynamically manage the role of each modality, allowing it to serve either as a generation or conditioning signal. This enables tasks such as text-to-video generation, X-conditioned video generation (where 'X' is a specific modality), and multi-modal video understanding (predicting depth, segmentation, etc., from RGB). The authors fine-tune a pre-trained text-to-video model and use expert models to generate pseudo labels for modalities lacking real-world paired data. Experiments across various tasks demonstrate the model's ability to generate high-quality, controllable video without requiring separate fine-tuning for each condition.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the unified framework for controllable video diffusion and understanding. While previous works have addressed either controllable generation or multi-modal generation separately, OmniVDiff attempts to combine these capabilities in a single model. The adaptive modality control strategy, which allows flexible management of generation vs. conditioning roles for each modality, is also a significant contribution. The idea of using pseudo-labels generated by expert models to augment training data, while not entirely new, is a practical solution given the scarcity of paired multi-modal video data. The approach of treating modalities in the color space for a joint distribution is worth noting.

*   **Significance:** If successful, OmniVDiff could significantly improve the flexibility and scalability of controllable video diffusion. The elimination of task-specific fine-tuning and dependence on external models would reduce computational costs and simplify the development process. The ability to perform video understanding within the same framework as generation could lead to more coherent and consistent results. Furthermore, the ability to support a broad range of modalities and tasks makes the framework potentially valuable for various downstream applications, such as video editing and video-to-video translation.
    The paper demonstrates some improvements over the baselines. However, the quantitative improvement is not very impressive. Furthermore, the qualitative results show improvement but may be subjective.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework is well-motivated, addressing a significant challenge in controllable video diffusion.
    *   The adaptive modality control strategy provides a flexible and efficient way to manage different modalities.
    *   The experiments are reasonably comprehensive, covering various tasks and modalities.
    *   The paper is clearly written and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on pseudo-labels introduces potential biases and artifacts. While the authors acknowledge this limitation, the impact on overall performance could be significant, particularly for segmentation. The reliance on pretrained models is also another issue.
    *   The quantitative improvements over existing methods, while present, are not overwhelmingly strong. This raises questions about the practical benefits of the proposed approach compared to simpler, task-specific solutions.
    *   The experiments primarily focus on a limited set of modalities (RGB, depth, segmentation, canny). The adaptability of the framework to entirely new or less common modalities could be further explored.
    *   The computational costs of training and inference are not thoroughly discussed. Diffusion models are known to be computationally expensive, and the overhead introduced by the unified framework could be a limitation in practice.
    *   The novelty of this work is somewhat incremental compared to a vast body of literature on diffusion models and conditional generation. While the combination of different aspects is commendable, the individual components are not groundbreaking.
    *   The paper does not explore more advanced video diffusion techniques.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    The paper could influence future research on unified frameworks for video generation and understanding. The adaptive modality control strategy and the use of pseudo-labels could be adopted by other researchers. However, the impact will depend on the ability of the framework to scale to a broader range of modalities and tasks while maintaining competitive performance. The video understanding component is another promising direction.

**Justification for Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6**. While the paper presents a novel and well-motivated framework for controllable video diffusion, its novelty is somewhat incremental, and the quantitative improvements over existing methods are not overwhelmingly strong. The reliance on pseudo-labels and the potential computational costs also represent limitations. However, the unified approach, the adaptive modality control strategy, and the potential for various downstream applications make this a valuable contribution to the field. The score reflects the promise of the framework and its potential influence on future research, while acknowledging the limitations and areas for improvement. The experimental results are sufficient but could be better. The method also relies on existing techniques.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### SteerMusic: Enhanced Musical Consistency for Zero-shot Text-Guided and Personalized Music Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10826v1)
- **Authors**: Xinlei Niu, Kin Wai Cheuk, Jing Zhang, Naoki Murata, Chieh-Hsin Lai, Michele Mancusi, Woosung Choi, Giorgio Fabbro, Wei-Hsiang Liao, Charles Patrick Martin, Yuki Mitsufuji
- **Abstract**: Music editing is an important step in music production, which has broad applications, including game development and film production. Most existing zero-shot text-guided methods rely on pretrained diffusion models by involving forward-backward diffusion processes for editing. However, these methods often struggle to maintain the music content consistency. Additionally, text instructions alone usually fail to accurately describe the desired music. In this paper, we propose two music editing methods that enhance the consistency between the original and edited music by leveraging score distillation. The first method, SteerMusic, is a coarse-grained zero-shot editing approach using delta denoising score. The second method, SteerMusic+, enables fine-grained personalized music editing by manipulating a concept token that represents a user-defined musical style. SteerMusic+ allows for the editing of music into any user-defined musical styles that cannot be achieved by the text instructions alone. Experimental results show that our methods outperform existing approaches in preserving both music content consistency and editing fidelity. User studies further validate that our methods achieve superior music editing quality. Audio examples are available on https://steermusic.pages.dev/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SteerMusic: Enhanced Musical Consistency for Zero-shot Text-Guided and Personalized Music Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces two novel music editing methods, SteerMusic and SteerMusic+, designed to improve musical consistency in zero-shot text-guided and personalized music editing scenarios. Both build on pre-trained diffusion models, leveraging score distillation techniques to maintain content from the original music during editing. SteerMusic focuses on coarse-grained, text-guided editing using delta denoising score (DDS). SteerMusic+ enables fine-grained personalized music editing by manipulating a concept token representing a user-defined musical style, going beyond what text instructions alone can achieve. Experimental results show that these methods outperform existing approaches regarding music content consistency and editing fidelity, validated through user studies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the application of score distillation (DDS specifically) to the task of zero-shot music editing. While DDS has been used in image editing, this is a reasonably novel adaptation to the music domain. SteerMusic+ further innovates by integrating a user-defined concept token, inspired by image personalization techniques, into the music editing process. The methods leverage well-established techinques, but the integration and adaption to the music domain are where the contribution is derived.  The idea of leveraging the DDS framework to address the inversion errors commonly found in diffusion-based editing pipelines is also a sound and logical contribution.
* **Significance:** Music editing is a practical and valuable task with applications in many areas. By improving the consistency and fidelity of zero-shot editing, the paper contributes to making these tools more usable and effective. The personalized editing aspect with concept tokens potentially opens up new possibilities for users to exert more granular control over the editing process. However, the experiments and user studies focus on relatively simple editing tasks. The general concept is valuable, and the proposed method addresses a real limitation, but its application and generalizability remains unclear.  The significance hinges on how well these methods can scale to more complex and nuanced editing scenarios.
* **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Real Problem:** The paper clearly identifies and tackles the issue of musical content inconsistency that is a major drawback in existing zero-shot music editing methods.
    *   **Sound Technical Approach:** Using DDS, score distillation, and the incorporation of concept tokens show intelligent use of existing techniques adapted to the music domain.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The paper provides quantitative metrics (FAD, LPAPS, CQT-1 PCC, CDPAM) and user studies to support its claims of superiority.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured, explaining the methods and their rationale in a coherent manner.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While the results are promising, the evaluation focuses on relatively simple editing tasks. It's unclear how well these methods would perform on more complex music, longer audio segments, or edits that involve more radical changes. It is unclear whether this contribution is limited to more controlled editing tasks.
    *   **Dependency on a Good PDM:** The SteerMusic+ method relies heavily on a well-trained personalized diffusion model (PDM). This introduces a dependency and potential performance bottleneck.  The training process of the PDM model is not a core focus of the paper.
    *   **Objective Metric Limitations:**  While metrics like FAD and LPAPS provide useful information, they may not fully capture the subjective nuances of music quality. The subjective assessment are limited.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The approach is based on the adaptation of exisiting well-known techniques, rather than introducing ground breaking innovation.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence future research by encouraging further exploration of score distillation and related techniques for music editing, as well as the integration of personalized concepts into these models.  It offers a solid baseline and demonstrates a clear path for improving consistency in diffusion-based music editing.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.  While the paper presents solid, well-executed research that addresses a relevant problem and introduces some clever adaptations of existing techniques, it does not represent a transformative breakthrough. The improvements are incremental but valuable, and the limitations concerning generalizability and dependency on PDMs prevent a higher score. It is well written, technically sound, and provides a compelling case for the proposed approach, and there is a clear contribution in the form of integrating these techniques with each other.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.SD
- **Score**: 7/10

### LayoutCoT: Unleashing the Deep Reasoning Potential of Large Language Models for Layout Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10829v1)
- **Authors**: Hengyu Shi, Junhao Su, Huansheng Ning, Xiaoming Wei, Jialin Gao
- **Abstract**: Conditional layout generation aims to automatically generate visually appealing and semantically coherent layouts from user-defined constraints. While recent methods based on generative models have shown promising results, they typically require substantial amounts of training data or extensive fine-tuning, limiting their versatility and practical applicability. Alternatively, some training-free approaches leveraging in-context learning with Large Language Models (LLMs) have emerged, but they often suffer from limited reasoning capabilities and overly simplistic ranking mechanisms, which restrict their ability to generate consistently high-quality layouts. To this end, we propose LayoutCoT, a novel approach that leverages the reasoning capabilities of LLMs through a combination of Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) techniques. Specifically, LayoutCoT transforms layout representations into a standardized serialized format suitable for processing by LLMs. A Layout-aware RAG is used to facilitate effective retrieval and generate a coarse layout by LLMs. This preliminary layout, together with the selected exemplars, is then fed into a specially designed CoT reasoning module for iterative refinement, significantly enhancing both semantic coherence and visual quality. We conduct extensive experiments on five public datasets spanning three conditional layout generation tasks. Experimental results demonstrate that LayoutCoT achieves state-of-the-art performance without requiring training or fine-tuning. Notably, our CoT reasoning module enables standard LLMs, even those without explicit deep reasoning abilities, to outperform specialized deep-reasoning models such as deepseek-R1, highlighting the potential of our approach in unleashing the deep reasoning capabilities of LLMs for layout generation tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LayoutCoT: Unleashing the Deep Reasoning Potential of Large Language Models for Layout Generation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LayoutCoT, a novel approach for conditional layout generation that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) in a training-free manner. The core idea is to enhance the reasoning capabilities of LLMs by combining Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and Chain-of-Thought (CoT) techniques.  Layout representations are serialized for LLM processing. A layout-aware RAG facilitates the retrieval of relevant layout examples, which are then used to generate a coarse initial layout. This coarse layout, along with the retrieved examples, is fed into a CoT reasoning module, which iteratively refines the layout through three distinct stages: initial element placement, size and position optimization, and fine-grained mathematical adjustments.  The approach is evaluated on five public datasets across three layout generation tasks, demonstrating state-of-the-art performance without requiring training or fine-tuning. Notably, the CoT module enables even standard LLMs to surpass specialized deep-reasoning models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its effective combination of RAG and CoT specifically tailored for layout generation. While RAG and CoT are established techniques, their application to layout problems, especially the design of a multi-stage CoT process that addresses specific aspects of layout refinement (placement, size adjustment, fine-tuning), is a contribution.  The layout-aware RAG component, utilizing LTSim for exemplar retrieval, also contributes to the system's effectiveness. However, the core components (RAG and CoT) are not entirely novel *per se*, but rather repurposed and specialized.

* **Significance:** The significance of the paper is potentially high.  The training-free nature of the approach is a major advantage.  Existing generative layout models often require large amounts of labeled data and extensive training, limiting their applicability. LayoutCoT offers a more flexible and versatile paradigm, allowing for layout generation across different tasks and datasets without needing retraining. The fact that the approach allows generic LLMs to outperform specialized deep-reasoning models suggests a powerful method for leveraging existing LLM capabilities, reducing the reliance on task-specific architectures.  The method could significantly lower the barrier to entry for automated layout generation. The clear decomposition into interpretable stages of reasoning is also valuable.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Training-free approach:**  Reduces the need for large datasets and extensive training.
    * **Effective use of LLMs:** Exploits the inherent reasoning capabilities of LLMs.
    * **RAG and CoT combination:** Combines the benefits of both techniques.
    * **Multi-stage CoT:** Addresses different aspects of layout design in a structured manner.
    * **Strong empirical results:**  Demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on several datasets.
    * **Clear problem decomposition:** Breaks down the layout generation process into manageable reasoning steps.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Reliance on LLMs:** Performance is inherently tied to the capabilities of the underlying LLM. Future LLM advances or limitations will directly affect LayoutCoT's effectiveness.
    * **Complexity:**  While training-free, the system involves several components (RAG, coarse generation, CoT), increasing overall complexity compared to a simple generative model.
    * **Generalizability Beyond Datasets Evaluated:** Although demonstrated on five datasets, there is the question of how well the method can generalize to completely novel tasks or dataset domains.
    * **Overlap Metric in Content-Aware Layout:** The paper acknowledges the suboptimal performance of the overlap metric. While they introduce a new metric to mitigate this, it raises concerns about evaluation robustness in that particular task.

* **Potential Influence:**  If proven robust and generalizable across diverse layout scenarios, LayoutCoT could inspire new research directions in training-free layout generation.  It may also drive the development of more specialized RAG and CoT techniques tailored for visual design tasks. The concept of decomposing complex layout tasks into simpler reasoning steps via CoT could be applied to other creative applications.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of layout generation. Its training-free nature and reliance on generic LLMs are compelling advantages. The combination of RAG and CoT, along with the carefully designed multi-stage reasoning process, demonstrates a thoughtful approach to leveraging LLM capabilities. The empirical results, showcasing state-of-the-art performance, further validate the effectiveness of the method.

However, the method relies on existing components (RAG, CoT), and has weaknesses with the content-aware layout task.  While the application is novel, it does not revolutionize the base techniques themselves. The dependence on LLM capabilities is also a limiting factor.

Therefore, based on a careful consideration of its novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Hallucination-Aware Generative Pretrained Transformer for Cooperative Aerial Mobility Control
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10831v1)
- **Authors**: Hyojun Ahn, Seungcheol Oh, Gyu Seon Kim, Soyi Jung, Soohyun Park, Joongheon Kim
- **Abstract**: This paper proposes SafeGPT, a two-tiered framework that integrates generative pretrained transformers (GPTs) with reinforcement learning (RL) for efficient and reliable unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) last-mile deliveries. In the proposed design, a Global GPT module assigns high-level tasks such as sector allocation, while an On-Device GPT manages real-time local route planning. An RL-based safety filter monitors each GPT decision and overrides unsafe actions that could lead to battery depletion or duplicate visits, effectively mitigating hallucinations. Furthermore, a dual replay buffer mechanism helps both the GPT modules and the RL agent refine their strategies over time. Simulation results demonstrate that SafeGPT achieves higher delivery success rates compared to a GPT-only baseline, while substantially reducing battery consumption and travel distance. These findings validate the efficacy of combining GPT-based semantic reasoning with formal safety guarantees, contributing a viable solution for robust and energy-efficient UAV logistics.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SafeGPT, a two-tiered framework designed to improve the safety and efficiency of UAVs for last-mile delivery. It combines generative pretrained transformers (GPTs) with reinforcement learning (RL) to handle dynamic environments and ensure safe operation within constraints like battery life.  A Global GPT module assigns high-level tasks, while On-Device GPTs manage local route planning. An RL-based safety filter overrides potentially unsafe actions suggested by the GPTs, mitigating the problem of hallucinations. A dual replay buffer mechanism helps both GPT modules and the RL agent to learn and refine their strategies. Simulation results indicate that SafeGPT achieves higher delivery success rates, reduces battery consumption, and decreases travel distance compared to a GPT-only baseline.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Integration:** The core strength of the paper lies in its novel integration of GPT and RL.  It addresses a crucial limitation of LLMs – their susceptibility to hallucinations – in the context of safety-critical applications (UAV control). Combining GPT for high-level planning with RL for safety and constraint satisfaction is a compelling architectural choice.
    *   **Practical Problem:** The paper tackles a relevant and important problem: efficient and safe UAV delivery.  The need for reliable and energy-efficient route planning in dynamic environments is well-established.
    *   **Two-Tiered Approach:**  The hierarchical GPT structure (Global and On-Device) seems like a logical way to distribute computational load and handle different levels of decision-making, especially considering the limited resources of UAVs.
    *   **Dual Replay Buffer:** The dual replay buffer is a clever mechanism that allows both the RL agent and the GPT modules to learn from past experiences, including instances where the GPT's suggestions were overridden due to safety concerns. This facilitates a synergistic learning process.
    *   **Clear Experimental Setup:** The experimental setup is clearly described, and the chosen metrics (success rate, battery consumption, travel distance) are relevant to the problem. The simulation environment is also clearly stated.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the integration of GPT and RL is novel, the individual components are not. RL-based safety filters and hierarchical planning have been explored in the context of robotics and autonomous systems previously. The novelty is primarily in the *specific combination* of these techniques.
    *   **GPT Selection Justification**: The paper mentions GPT-40 and GPT-40mini without strong rationale. It should elaborate on the trade-offs made in GPT model selection.
    *   **Simulation Realism:**  The description of the simulation environment is somewhat limited.  The paper could benefit from more details on how dynamic elements (e.g., weather conditions, traffic) are modeled and how realistic the battery consumption model is. A discussion of how the simulated urban environment reflects real-world complexities is needed.
    *   **Hallucination Mitigation Measurement**: Although the hallucination issues associated with the GPT is stated in the paper. This is not adequately measured through the hallucination count in a way that reflects real operational constraints/deviations.
    *   **Lack of Comparison with Other Approaches:** The paper compares SafeGPT to a GPT-only baseline, which is a reasonable starting point. However, it would be stronger if it also included a comparison to traditional RL methods or other approaches for safe autonomous navigation. This would allow for a more thorough evaluation of the benefits of the GPT-RL hybrid approach.
    *   **Generalizability**: The evaluation is performed in a relatively simple simulated environment. The paper should address the scalability and generalizability of SafeGPT to larger, more complex, and realistic environments with more UAVs and dynamic factors. The specific prompts employed for Global and On-Device GPT need to be generalizable and not overfit to the setup.
    *   **Real-world Deployment Feasibility:** It is critical to discuss the feasibility of deploying this system in the real world and potential challenges involved in real-world implementation.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The paper provides a promising framework for addressing the challenges of safe and efficient UAV operation.  The SafeGPT approach has the potential to be a valuable contribution to the field of autonomous robotics and logistics.
    *   The ideas presented in the paper could inspire further research on combining LLMs with formal safety guarantees in other safety-critical applications.
    *   If the framework can be successfully scaled to more complex environments and validated with real-world experiments, it could have a significant impact on the future of UAV-based delivery services.

**Score:** 6/10

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and relevant integration of GPT and RL for safe UAV control, which is a worthwhile contribution to the field. The two-tiered approach and dual replay buffer mechanism are interesting design choices. However, the paper has limitations in the realism of the simulation environment, comparison with other methods, and a generalizability study that lowers the impact and real-world significance. Therefore, a score of 6 reflects a good, solid research effort with promising results, but requiring more extensive validation and comparison to achieve a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Moving Beyond Next-Token Prediction: Transformers are Context-Sensitive Language Generators
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10845v1)
- **Authors**: Phill Kyu Rhee
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs), powered by Transformers, have demonstrated human-like intelligence capabilities, yet their underlying mechanisms remain poorly understood. This paper presents a novel framework for interpreting LLMs as probabilistic left context-sensitive languages (CSLs) generators. We hypothesize that Transformers can be effectively decomposed into three fundamental components: context windows, attention mechanisms, and autoregressive generation frameworks. This decomposition allows for the development of more flexible and interpretable computational models, moving beyond the traditional view of attention and autoregression as inseparable processes. We argue that next-token predictions can be understood as probabilistic, dynamic approximations of left CSL production rules, providing an intuitive explanation for how simple token predictions can yield human-like intelligence outputs. Given that all CSLs are left context-sensitive (Penttonen, 1974), we conclude that Transformers stochastically approximate CSLs, which are widely recognized as models of human-like intelligence. This interpretation bridges the gap between Formal Language Theory and the observed generative power of Transformers, laying a foundation for future advancements in generative AI theory and applications. Our novel perspective on Transformer architectures will foster a deeper understanding of LLMs and their future potentials.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel framework for understanding Large Language Models (LLMs) as stochastic generators of left context-sensitive languages (CSLs). It argues that Transformers, the architecture powering LLMs, can be decomposed into three key components: context windows, attention mechanisms, and autoregressive generation.  The authors hypothesize that next-token predictions can be viewed as probabilistic approximations of left CSL production rules.  Given the equivalence between CSLs and left CSLs, the paper concludes that Transformers stochastically approximate CSLs, bridging formal language theory with the observed generative power of LLMs. The paper emphasizes a focus on generative processes rather than internal probing and aims to provide a more intuitive understanding of how simple token predictions lead to human-like intelligence.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of framing LLMs as stochastic CSL generators is relatively novel. While prior work explores the relationship between Transformers and formal languages, this paper makes a specific connection to left CSLs and highlights a decomposition of the architecture to support this interpretation. The attempt to explicitly link next-token prediction with the production rules of CSLs offers a fresh perspective.
* **Significance:** If substantiated, this framework could have significant implications. By connecting LLMs to CSLs, the paper potentially opens avenues for applying formal language theory to analyze and understand the limitations and capabilities of LLMs more rigorously. This also hints at new optimization strategies for LLMs.  It provides an explanation for the human-like capabilities of these language generators.
* **Strengths:**
    * The paper provides a clear and well-structured argument.
    * The decomposition of Transformers into three components is a useful analytical tool.
    * The paper makes explicit connections between Transformer mechanisms (context window, attention) and formal language constructs.
    * It acknowledges and addresses existing critiques and limitations of Transformers from a formal language perspective.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * The argument relies heavily on the theoretical equivalence between CSLs and left CSLs and the claim that human languages belong to the CSL class, which may still have some debate (albeit less now than in the past). The paper doesn't provide novel empirical evidence to *directly* support its hypothesis; it's more of a theoretical reinterpretation.
    * The "decomposition" of transformers, while conceptually useful, may be an oversimplification of the complex interactions within these models.  The attention mechanism, in particular, is intimately involved in both context processing *and* next-token prediction, making a clean separation difficult.
    * The paper does not provide sufficient detail on how *specific* attention patterns or context window configurations map to specific CSL production rules. Examples would be useful. The translation is conceptual, not demonstrated. The paper provides insight, but not direct empirical methods to leverage the insights.
    * There isn't a clear path outlined for how this CSL framework *directly* improves LLM design or addresses current limitations. It's more of a re-framing than a solution.
    * The paper assumes the reader is comfortable with concepts from both formal language theory and deep learning.
* **Impact:**
    * The potential impact depends on whether this framework inspires further research and practical applications. The value is primarily in its conceptual reframing of LLMs, which could lead to new ways of analyzing and improving them. However, without strong empirical support and practical implications, its influence might be limited.
* **Justification:**
The paper's strength lies in its relatively novel theoretical perspective and its potential to bridge formal language theory and LLMs. However, its lack of strong empirical backing and concrete implications for LLM development limit its immediate impact. The reinterpretation is helpful, and offers a novel lens for analysis, but requires considerable work to translate into actual practical guidance and methods to derive the full value.
Therefore, a score of 6 is assigned.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enhancing Features in Long-tailed Data Using Large Vision Mode
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10852v1)
- **Authors**: Pengxiao Han, Changkun Ye, Jinguang Tong, Cuicui Jiang, Jie Hong, Li Fang, Xuesong Li
- **Abstract**: Language-based foundation models, such as large language models (LLMs) or large vision-language models (LVLMs), have been widely studied in long-tailed recognition. However, the need for linguistic data is not applicable to all practical tasks. In this study, we aim to explore using large vision models (LVMs) or visual foundation models (VFMs) to enhance long-tailed data features without any language information. Specifically, we extract features from the LVM and fuse them with features in the baseline network's map and latent space to obtain the augmented features. Moreover, we design several prototype-based losses in the latent space to further exploit the potential of the augmented features. In the experimental section, we validate our approach on two benchmark datasets: ImageNet-LT and iNaturalist2018.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper tackles the long-tailed recognition problem by leveraging large vision models (LVMs) like the Segment Anything Model (SAM) to augment features in a baseline classification network. Unlike many existing approaches that rely on language information or specialized network architectures, this method uses only visual data. The core idea is to extract features from SAM and fuse them with baseline network features in both the feature map and latent spaces.  Additionally, the paper introduces a prototype-based loss function that dynamically adjusts learning weights for head and tail classes, encouraging compact clustering for head classes and promoting diversity for tail classes. The approach is validated on ImageNet-LT and iNaturalist2018 datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel approach to long-tailed recognition by directly incorporating features from a large vision model (LVM) like SAM without relying on linguistic data. This is particularly useful in scenarios where language information is unavailable or less relevant.  The fusion of SAM features at different levels (feature map and latent space) and the dynamically adjusted prototype loss are also relatively novel contributions.
    *   **Technical Soundness:** The approach is technically well-defined, with clear explanations of the feature extraction, fusion, and loss function. The experiments appear to be carefully conducted, and the results are clearly presented.
    *   **Experimental Results:** The experiments on ImageNet-LT and iNaturalist2018 demonstrate consistent improvements over several baselines, including standard cross-entropy, label shift, and even ProCo. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each component, like the feature fusion and the prototype-based losses.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The long-tailed recognition problem is a significant challenge in many real-world applications. By demonstrating the effectiveness of LVMs in this context, the paper offers a potentially practical solution.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the results show improvements, the gains, while consistent, are sometimes incremental relative to more advanced approaches. The jump from standard CE to the final proposed method is decent but isn't necessarily paradigm-shifting in magnitude.
    *   **Limited Scope of LVM Utilization:** The paper primarily uses SAM as a feature extractor.  While effective, it doesn't fully exploit the potential of SAM for tasks such as instance segmentation or object localization that could inform the classification task more richly. Simply fusing features might not be the most optimal integration strategy.
    *   **Lack of Deeper Analysis:** While ablation studies are presented, the paper could benefit from a deeper analysis of *why* the proposed method works. For example, a visualization of the feature space or a more detailed analysis of the learned prototypes would be helpful. What specific types of tail classes benefit the most? How is the prototype learning stabilized?
    *   **Computational Cost:** The incorporation of SAM inevitably increases the computational cost. The paper doesn't explicitly discuss the computational overhead and compare it with alternative approaches. This is crucial in practice. The method introduces extra layers, which could potentially increase the training time and inference time.
    *   **Parameter Sensitivity:** The paper mentions several hyperparameters (e.g., weight of prototype loss, thresholds for head/tail classes). A more detailed analysis of the sensitivity of the results to these parameters would enhance the robustness of the findings.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The work highlights the potential of LVMs (specifically SAM) for long-tailed recognition without relying on language data. This direction is valuable because it opens doors for applications where linguistic information is unavailable or irrelevant.
    *   The idea of feature fusion at different levels (feature map and latent space) is a good exploration and is shown to be effective.
    *   The paper's detailed ablation study provides useful insights into the relative contributions of each component.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper provides a valuable contribution by exploring a novel approach to long-tailed recognition using LVMs in a language-agnostic manner.  The feature fusion strategy and prototype-based loss are interesting and show improvement over baselines. However, the improvements are incremental in some cases, and the paper could benefit from a deeper analysis of *why* the method works and a discussion of the computational cost. The scope of SAM utilization is relatively limited, and the paper lacks a thorough hyperparameter sensitivity analysis. These limitations prevent the paper from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### PT-Mark: Invisible Watermarking for Text-to-image Diffusion Models via Semantic-aware Pivotal Tuning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10853v1)
- **Authors**: Yaopeng Wang, Huiyu Xu, Zhibo Wang, Jiacheng Du, Zhichao Li, Yiming Li, Qiu Wang, Kui Ren
- **Abstract**: Watermarking for diffusion images has drawn considerable attention due to the widespread use of text-to-image diffusion models and the increasing need for their copyright protection. Recently, advanced watermarking techniques, such as Tree Ring, integrate watermarks by embedding traceable patterns (e.g., Rings) into the latent distribution during the diffusion process. Such methods disrupt the original semantics of the generated images due to the inevitable distribution shift caused by the watermarks, thereby limiting their practicality, particularly in digital art creation. In this work, we present Semantic-aware Pivotal Tuning Watermarks (PT-Mark), a novel invisible watermarking method that preserves both the semantics of diffusion images and the traceability of the watermark. PT-Mark preserves the original semantics of the watermarked image by gradually aligning the generation trajectory with the original (pivotal) trajectory while maintaining the traceable watermarks during whole diffusion denoising process. To achieve this, we first compute the salient regions of the watermark at each diffusion denoising step as a spatial prior to identify areas that can be aligned without disrupting the watermark pattern. Guided by the region, we then introduce an additional pivotal tuning branch that optimizes the text embedding to align the semantics while preserving the watermarks. Extensive evaluations demonstrate that PT-Mark can preserve the original semantics of the diffusion images while integrating robust watermarks. It achieves a 10% improvement in the performance of semantic preservation (i.e., SSIM, PSNR, and LPIPS) compared to state-of-the-art watermarking methods, while also showing comparable robustness against real-world perturbations and four times greater efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PT-Mark: Invisible Watermarking for Text-to-image Diffusion Models via Semantic-aware Pivotal Tuning":

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel watermarking technique called "Semantic-aware Pivotal Tuning Watermark (PT-Mark)" for text-to-image diffusion models.  The core idea is to preserve the original semantics of the image while embedding a robust watermark. PT-Mark works by aligning the generation trajectory of the watermarked image with the original (pivotal) trajectory during the diffusion denoising process. It identifies salient regions of the watermark using a segmentation network as a spatial prior and then uses a pivotal tuning branch to optimize the text embedding, aligning the semantics while preserving the watermark. The paper claims significant improvements in semantic preservation compared to existing watermarking methods, along with comparable robustness against real-world perturbations and increased efficiency.  The method is presented as a plug-and-play module that can be integrated with existing watermarking techniques.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The idea of explicitly considering and preserving image semantics during the watermarking process in diffusion models is a valuable contribution. The use of a segmentation network to identify salient watermark regions and the pivotal tuning branch for semantic alignment is also novel.  While null-text inversion has been explored, its application within a watermarking framework and the specific loss functions used to guide the optimization are new. The claim of a plug-and-play module that can enhance existing techniques adds to its novelty.
*   **Significance:** Copyright protection for AI-generated images is a growing concern, making this research relevant and timely. If the method effectively balances semantic preservation and watermark robustness, it could have a significant impact on the practical application of watermarking in diffusion models. The reported improvements in image quality metrics (PSNR, SSIM, etc.) and efficiency are compelling.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Semantic Preservation:** The explicit focus on maintaining image semantics is a key strength, addressing a major limitation of existing watermarking methods.
    *   **Robustness:**  The reported robustness against common image perturbations is important for practical deployment.
    *   **Efficiency:**  The claimed efficiency gains compared to other methods are attractive.
    *   **Plug-and-Play Capability:** The ability to easily integrate with existing techniques is a significant advantage, potentially widening its applicability.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper includes quantitative metrics, qualitative examples, and ablation studies, providing a reasonably thorough evaluation.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Segmentation Network:** The reliance on a pretrained segmentation network introduces a potential dependency and may limit its performance on images with drastically different characteristics than those the segmentation network was trained on. More details regarding the segmentation networks training and its interaction with the rest of the process would be beneficial.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The success of the method appears heavily reliant on careful tuning of hyperparameters, such as the weights for semantic alignment and watermark preservation losses. This could make it difficult to use in practice without significant experimentation.
    *   **Limited Scope of Perturbations:**  While the paper evaluates robustness against common image perturbations, it may not fully address more sophisticated or targeted attacks that could potentially remove or disable the watermark. Further robustness against adaptive watermark removal attacks would improve the robustness of the approach.
    *   **Subjective Quality:** While the quantitative improvements seem good, more subjective analysis on the output watermarked images would be beneficial.
    *   **Scalability Analysis:** While runtime analysis has been completed, scalability analysis for varying resolutions or larger batch sizes has been omitted.

**Overall Assessment and Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of image watermarking for diffusion models. The explicit focus on semantic preservation addresses a key limitation of existing methods, and the proposed PT-Mark technique offers a promising approach. While the method has weaknesses, its strengths in semantic preservation, robustness, and potential efficiency make it an important advancement.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Bringing together invertible UNets with invertible attention modules for memory-efficient diffusion models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10883v1)
- **Authors**: Karan Jain, Mohammad Nayeem Teli
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have recently gained state of the art performance on many image generation tasks. However, most models require significant computational resources to achieve this. This becomes apparent in the application of medical image synthesis due to the 3D nature of medical datasets like CT-scans, MRIs, electron microscope, etc. In this paper we propose a novel architecture for a single GPU memory-efficient training for diffusion models for high dimensional medical datasets. The proposed model is built by using an invertible UNet architecture with invertible attention modules. This leads to the following two contributions: 1. denoising diffusion models and thus enabling memory usage to be independent of the dimensionality of the dataset, and 2. reducing the energy usage during training. While this new model can be applied to a multitude of image generation tasks, we showcase its memory-efficiency on the 3D BraTS2020 dataset leading to up to 15\% decrease in peak memory consumption during training with comparable results to SOTA while maintaining the image quality.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Invertible Diffusion Model (IDM), a novel architecture designed for memory-efficient training of diffusion models, particularly for high-dimensional medical datasets like 3D MRI scans. IDM leverages invertible U-Nets with invertible attention modules. This approach allows activations to be reconstructed rather than stored during backpropagation, reducing memory consumption. The authors claim this leads to lower energy usage and allows training on single GPUs, which are often limited in memory. They validate IDM on the BraTS2020 MRI dataset, showing a decrease in peak memory consumption with comparable image quality to existing state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the **combination** of invertible U-Nets with invertible attention specifically tailored for diffusion models used in 3D medical imaging. While invertible networks and attention mechanisms are individually established, their integration within a diffusion model, with a focus on reducing memory footprint *specifically in the context of 3D medical data*, provides a new contribution. The extension of invertible attention to 3D is also a specific, incremental advancement. The data-dependent, learnable upsampling/downsampling via invertible layers in iUNets is another key component.

*   **Significance:** The significance is in making diffusion models more accessible and sustainable for resource-intensive 3D medical image generation. Diffusion models achieve SOTA results, but can be difficult to implement in 3D, which medical imaging often calls for due to the high memory demand. Addressing memory limitations opens avenues for researchers and practitioners with limited resources (e.g., single GPU setups) to experiment with and apply these powerful generative models. The reduced energy consumption aligns with growing concerns about the environmental impact of AI, further adding to the significance.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the memory bottleneck in training diffusion models for 3D medical images.
    *   **Technical Solution:** The architectural choices are well-motivated, and the integration of invertible components is presented logically.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The BraTS2020 dataset is a recognized benchmark. The memory usage results show the efficacy of IDM, and energy savings show that IDM is more efficient than SOTA in terms of energy.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** Evaluation metrics (PSNR, SSIM, MAE, FLOPs, Memory, Temperature) give a complete picture of the models' performance.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Incremental Improvement:** While the combination is novel, the individual components (invertible UNets, attention) are not. This limits the groundbreaking impact of the architecture.
    *   **SSIM Discrepancy:** The lower SSIM values, despite comparable PSNR, raise concerns about the structural fidelity of the generated images. This needs further investigation and may limit its applicability to tasks requiring high structural detail.
    *   **Limited Comparison:** The comparison in the paper (regarding data types) is not in terms of SOTA methods applied to the same tasks. While memory footprint is reported against various techniques like gradient checkpointing, a more detailed comparison against directly comparable methods would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Qualitative Evaluation:** The qualitative evaluation shown is limited to one figure with two slices. It's not clear if the model preserves the tumors when generating new data.
    *   **Generalizability:** The model's effectiveness is primarily demonstrated on BraTS2020. While promising, extending the approach to datasets with different characteristics (e.g., different modalities, image sizes, or anatomical structures) would bolster confidence in its generalizability.

*   **Potential Impact:** If IDM's demonstrated benefits extend to a broader range of medical imaging applications, it could significantly lower the barrier to entry for using diffusion models in healthcare, accelerating research and development. The potential for energy-efficient AI development is also important.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a valuable contribution by addressing a practical limitation (memory consumption) in a relevant application domain (3D medical image generation using diffusion models). The integration of invertible architectures, while not a completely revolutionary concept, is well-executed and addresses memory bottlenecks effectively. However, the incremental nature of the improvements, the discrepancy in SSIM, the lack of comprehensive data/qualitative analysis and the specific focus on one data set, limits the overall impact and generalizability of the proposed method. A slightly higher score of 7 would be justified if the qualitative images showed preserved tumors and results on SynapseMNIST3D.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### Exploring Persona-dependent LLM Alignment for the Moral Machine Experiment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10886v1)
- **Authors**: Jiseon Kim, Jea Kwon, Luiz Felipe Vecchietti, Alice Oh, Meeyoung Cha
- **Abstract**: Deploying large language models (LLMs) with agency in real-world applications raises critical questions about how these models will behave. In particular, how will their decisions align with humans when faced with moral dilemmas? This study examines the alignment between LLM-driven decisions and human judgment in various contexts of the moral machine experiment, including personas reflecting different sociodemographics. We find that the moral decisions of LLMs vary substantially by persona, showing greater shifts in moral decisions for critical tasks than humans. Our data also indicate an interesting partisan sorting phenomenon, where political persona predominates the direction and degree of LLM decisions. We discuss the ethical implications and risks associated with deploying these models in applications that involve moral decisions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**
